     1→# CloudFront Deployment Guide for MCP Server
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→The MCP server is deployed on `simplemap.safecast.org` which uses AWS CloudFront as a CDN. Understanding the traffic flow is critical for proper deployment.
     6→
     7→## Infrastructure
     8→
     9→**Server IP:** 65.108.24.131
    10→**Domain:** simplemap.safecast.org
    11→**CDN:** AWS CloudFront
    12→
    13→## Traffic Flow
    14→
    15→```
    16→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    17→│              HTTPS Traffic (Ports 80/443)               │
    18→│                    ✅ Works via Domain                   │
    19→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    20→  User/AI Client
    21→       ↓
    22→  simplemap.safecast.org (DNS → CloudFront)
    23→       ↓
    24→  CloudFront Distribution
    25→       ↓
    26→  Apache Reverse Proxy (on 65.108.24.131)
    27→       ↓
    28→  MCP Server (localhost:3333)
    29→       ↓
    30→  PostgreSQL Database (localhost:5432)
    31→
    32→
    33→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    34→│               SSH/Rsync Traffic (Port 22)               │
    35→│             ❌ MUST Use IP Address Directly              │
    36→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    37→  Developer Machine
    38→       ↓
    39→  65.108.24.131 (Direct IP - bypasses CloudFront)
    40→       ↓
    41→  SSH Server on 65.108.24.131
    42→```
    43→
    44→## Critical: SSH and CloudFront
    45→
    46→**CloudFront only handles HTTP/HTTPS traffic** (ports 80/443). It does NOT forward:
    47→- SSH (port 22)
    48→- Database connections (port 5432)
    49→- Any other TCP traffic
    50→
    51→### The Problem
    52→
    53→If you try to SSH using the domain name:
    54→```bash
    55→ssh root@simplemap.safecast.org  # ❌ Will FAIL
    56→```
    57→
    58→What happens:
    59→1. DNS resolves `simplemap.safecast.org` to CloudFront's IP addresses
    60→2. Your SSH client tries to connect to CloudFront's servers on port 22
    61→3. CloudFront doesn't accept SSH connections
    62→4. Connection fails or times out
    63→
    64→### The Solution
    65→
    66→Always use the IP address for SSH/rsync:
    67→```bash
    68→ssh root@65.108.24.131  # ✅ Correct
    69→scp file.txt root@65.108.24.131:/path/  # ✅ Correct
    70→rsync -avz ./local/ root@65.108.24.131:/remote/  # ✅ Correct
    71→```
    72→
    73→## Code Compatibility
    74→
    75→**Good news:** The MCP server code requires **NO modifications** to work with CloudFront.
    76→
    77→### Why It Works
    78→
    79→1. **HTTPS URLs use domain name** - CloudFront forwards these correctly
    80→   - `MCP_BASE_URL=https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp` ✅
    81→   - API fallback: `https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/radiation` ✅
    82→   - Swagger UI: `https://simplemap.safecast.org/docs/` ✅
    83→
    84→2. **Database uses localhost** - Not affected by CloudFront
    85→   - `DATABASE_URL=postgres://...@localhost:5432/safecast` ✅
    86→
    87→3. **Server listens on localhost** - Apache proxies requests
    88→   - MCP listens on `localhost:3333` ✅
    89→   - Apache forwards from CloudFront to localhost ✅
    90→
    91→## GitHub Actions Deployment
    92→
    93→The workflow has been updated to separate SSH host from HTTPS URLs.
    94→
    95→### Required Secrets
    96→
    97→| Secret | Value | Purpose |
    98→|--------|-------|---------|
    99→| `SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` | Your SSH private key | Authentication for deployment |
   100→| `MAP_SERVER_HOST` | **`65.108.24.131`** | Server IP for SSH/rsync |
   101→
   102→**⚠️ CRITICAL:** `MAP_SERVER_HOST` must be the **IP address** (65.108.24.131), not the domain name.
   103→
   104→### How the Workflow Works
   105→
   106→```yaml
   107→env:
   108→  MAP_SERVER_HOST: ${{ secrets.MAP_SERVER_HOST }}  # IP: 65.108.24.131
   109→  MAP_SERVER_DOMAIN: simplemap.safecast.org         # Hardcoded domain
   110→
   111→# SSH uses IP address
   112→SSH="ssh -i ~/.ssh/deploy_key root@$MAP_SERVER_HOST"  # Uses 65.108.24.131
   113→
   114→# HTTPS URL uses domain name
   115→ENV_FILE="MCP_BASE_URL=https://$MAP_SERVER_DOMAIN/mcp"  # Uses simplemap.safecast.org
   116→```
   117→
   118→This ensures:
   119→- ✅ SSH/rsync connects to the IP (bypasses CloudFront)
   120→- ✅ HTTPS URLs use the domain (goes through CloudFront)
   121→- ✅ Users access the MCP via CloudFront (fast, global CDN)
   122→- ✅ Deployment works correctly
   123→
   124→## Manual Deployment
   125→
   126→When deploying manually, always use the IP address:
   127→
   128→```bash
   129→cd go
   130→GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 go build -o safecast-mcp ./cmd/mcp-server/
   131→
   132→# ✅ Correct - Use IP address
   133→scp safecast-mcp root@65.108.24.131:/root/safecast-mcp-server/
   134→ssh root@65.108.24.131 "systemctl restart safecast-mcp"
   135→
   136→# ❌ Wrong - Domain won't work for SSH
   137→scp safecast-mcp root@simplemap.safecast.org:/root/safecast-mcp-server/  # FAILS!
   138→```
   139→
   140→## Verifying Deployment
   141→
   142→After deployment, verify both SSH access and HTTPS endpoints:
   143→
   144→### 1. SSH Access (Direct IP)
   145→```bash
   146→# ✅ This should work
   147→ssh root@65.108.24.131 "systemctl status safecast-mcp"
   148→
   149→# ❌ This will fail
   150→ssh root@simplemap.safecast.org "systemctl status safecast-mcp"
   151→```
   152→
   153→### 2. HTTPS Endpoints (Through CloudFront)
   154→```bash
   155→# ✅ All of these should work via domain
   156→curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/docs/
   157→curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/radiation?lat=37.42&lon=141.03&limit=5
   158→
   159→# Test MCP endpoint
   160→curl -X POST https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http \
   161→  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
   162→  -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"initialize","params":{"protocolVersion":"2025-03-26","capabilities":{},"clientInfo":{"name":"test","version":"1.0"}}}'
   163→```
   164→
   165→### 3. Check CloudFront Headers
   166→```bash
   167→curl -I https://simplemap.safecast.org/docs/
   168→# Look for CloudFront headers:
   169→# x-cache: Hit from cloudfront
   170→# x-amz-cf-pop: NRT57-P1
   171→# via: 1.1 xxxxx.cloudfront.net (CloudFront)
   172→```
   173→
   174→## Troubleshooting
   175→
   176→### Problem: SSH Connection Refused
   177→
   178→**Symptom:**
   179→```
   180→ssh: connect to host simplemap.safecast.org port 22: Connection refused
   181→```
   182→
   183→**Cause:** Trying to SSH to the domain name (CloudFront IP) instead of the server IP.
   184→
   185→**Solution:** Use IP address:
   186→```bash
   187→ssh root@65.108.24.131
   188→```
   189→
   190→### Problem: GitHub Actions Deploy Fails with SSH Error
   191→
   192→**Symptom:**
   193→```
   194→Permission denied (publickey)
   195→Host key verification failed
   196→```
   197→
   198→**Cause:** `MAP_SERVER_HOST` secret is set to domain name instead of IP.
   199→
   200→**Solution:** Update GitHub secret:
   201→1. Go to repository Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions
   202→2. Update `MAP_SERVER_HOST` to `65.108.24.131`
   203→3. Re-run the workflow
   204→
   205→### Problem: MCP Endpoints Return 502 Bad Gateway
   206→
   207→**Symptom:**
   208→```bash
   209→curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   210→# Returns: 502 Bad Gateway
   211→```
   212→
   213→**Cause:** MCP server not running or Apache proxy not configured.
   214→
   215→**Check:**
   216→```bash
   217→# SSH to server (use IP)
   218→ssh root@65.108.24.131
   219→
   220→# Check if MCP is running
   221→systemctl status safecast-mcp
   222→
   223→# Check if it's listening
   224→ss -tlnp | grep 3333
   225→
   226→# Check Apache proxy config
   227→grep -r "ProxyPass.*mcp" /etc/apache2/
   228→```
   229→
   230→### Problem: Database Connection Errors
   231→
   232→**Symptom:** MCP server logs show database connection errors.
   233→
   234→**Cause:** `DATABASE_URL` in `/root/safecast-mcp-server/.env` is incorrect.
   235→
   236→**Check:**
   237→```bash
   238→ssh root@65.108.24.131
   239→cat /root/safecast-mcp-server/.env
   240→
   241→# Should show:
   242→# DATABASE_URL=postgres://mcp_readonly:PASSWORD@localhost:5432/safecast
   243→# MCP_BASE_URL=https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp
   244→# REDACTED.duckdb
   245→```
   246→
   247→**Important:** Database connection must use `localhost`, not an external IP or domain.
   248→
   249→## Benefits of CloudFront Setup
   250→
   251→### For End Users
   252→- **Faster response times** - Content cached at edge locations worldwide
   253→- **Lower latency** - Tokyo, Europe, US users all get fast access
   254→- **Better reliability** - DDoS protection, automatic failover
   255→- **HTTPS security** - Free SSL certificate, modern TLS
   256→
   257→### For Deployment
   258→- **Direct server access** - SSH still works via IP address
   259→- **Optimal database performance** - MCP connects to localhost (no network overhead)
   260→- **Transparent operation** - MCP server code unchanged
   261→- **Cache control** - API responses not cached (dynamic data)
   262→
   263→## Environment Variables
   264→
   265→The MCP server uses these environment variables (set in `.env` file):
   266→
   267→```bash
   268→# Base URL for SSE transport - uses domain (goes through CloudFront)
   269→MCP_BASE_URL=https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp
   270→
   271→# Database connection - uses localhost (direct connection)
   272→DATABASE_URL=postgres://mcp_readonly:PASSWORD@localhost:5432/safecast
   273→
   274→# DuckDB analytics - local file path
   275→REDACTED.duckdb
   276→```
   277→
   278→These are automatically set by the GitHub Actions workflow.
   279→
   280→## Apache Proxy Configuration
   281→
   282→The Apache configuration on the server proxies CloudFront requests to the MCP server:
   283→
   284→```apache
   285→<VirtualHost *:443>
   286→    ServerName simplemap.safecast.org
   287→
   288→    # MCP Server Proxy
   289→    ProxyPass /mcp http://localhost:3333/mcp
   290→    ProxyPassReverse /mcp http://localhost:3333/mcp
   291→    ProxyPass /mcp-http http://localhost:3333/mcp-http
   292→    ProxyPassReverse /mcp-http http://localhost:3333/mcp-http
   293→
   294→    # REST API and Swagger UI
   295→    ProxyPass /docs/ http://localhost:3333/docs/
   296→    ProxyPassReverse /docs/ http://localhost:3333/docs/
   297→    ProxyPass /api/ http://localhost:3333/api/
   298→    ProxyPassReverse /api/ http://localhost:3333/api/
   299→
   300→    # ... other configuration ...
   301→</VirtualHost>
   302→```
   303→
   304→## Summary
   305→
   306→### ✅ Correct Usage
   307→
   308→```bash
   309→# SSH/Deployment - Use IP
   310→ssh root@65.108.24.131
   311→scp file root@65.108.24.131:/path/
   312→rsync -avz ./dir/ root@65.108.24.131:/path/
   313→
   314→# HTTPS/API Access - Use domain (goes through CloudFront)
   315→curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/mcp-http
   316→curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/docs/
   317→curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/radiation?lat=37.42&lon=141.03
   318→```
   319→
   320→### ❌ Incorrect Usage
   321→
   322→```bash
   323→# SSH with domain - WILL FAIL
   324→ssh root@simplemap.safecast.org  # CloudFront can't handle SSH!
   325→
   326→# Hardcoding IP in HTTPS URLs - BAD PRACTICE
   327→curl http://65.108.24.131:3333/mcp-http  # Bypasses CloudFront, no caching
   328→```

> AGENT

Based on the documentation, I can confirm the issue. Here's what I found:

## The Problem

The [CLOUDFRONT_DEPLOYMENT.md](docs/CLOUDFRONT_DEPLOYMENT.md) shows that Apache is correctly configured to proxy `/api/` requests:

```apache
ProxyPass /api/ http://localhost:3333/api/
ProxyPassReverse /api/ http://localhost:3333/api/
```

However, **CloudFront needs a cache behavior configured** for the `/api/*` path pattern, otherwise CloudFront will serve a default error page (HTML) instead of forwarding requests to your Apache origin.

## What Needs to Be Configured

You need to add a CloudFront cache behavior with these settings:

**Path Pattern:** `/api/*`

**Origin:** Same origin as `/mcp-http` and `/docs/*` (your Apache server at 65.108.24.131)

**Cache Settings:**
- Cache Policy: `CachingDisabled` (or TTL = 0)
- Origin Request Policy: Forward all query strings, headers, and cookies
- Viewer Protocol Policy: Redirect HTTP to HTTPS

**This matches the existing behaviors for:**
- `/mcp-http` (MCP server endpoint)
- `/docs/*` (Swagger UI)

## Verification

Unfortunately, I don't have direct access to your AWS CloudFront console from this repository. The CloudFront configuration would need to be done through:

1. **AWS Console** - CloudFront → Distributions → Select distribution → Behaviors → Create behavior
2. **Terraform/CloudFormation** - If you have infrastructure as code (I don't see any in the repo)
3. **AWS CLI** - Via command line

Would you like me to:
1. Create documentation for the exact CloudFront cache behavior settings needed?
2. Check if there are any AWS configuration files I might have missed in the repository?

```
server {
    server_name simplemap.safecast.org;
    
    # For Let's Encrypt verification
    location /.well-known/acme-challenge/ {
        root /var/www/html;
    }
    
    # MCP Server endpoints
    location /mcp {
        proxy_pass http://localhost:3333;
        proxy_http_version 1.1;
        proxy_set_header Upgrade $http_upgrade;
        proxy_set_header Connection "upgrade";
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;
        proxy_read_timeout 86400;
    }
    
    location /mcp-http {
        proxy_pass http://localhost:3333;
        proxy_http_version 1.1;
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;
    }
    
    # MCP REST API documentation
    location /docs/ {
        proxy_pass http://localhost:3333/docs/;
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;
    }
    
    # MCP REST API endpoints
    location /api/mcp/ {
        proxy_pass http://localhost:3333/api/;
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;
    }
    
    # Map server (including /api/auth/* and everything else)
    location / {
        proxy_pass http://localhost:8765;
        proxy_http_version 1.1;
        proxy_set_header Upgrade $http_upgrade;
        proxy_set_header Connection "upgrade";
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;
        client_max_body_size 100M;
    }

    listen [::]:443 ssl ipv6only=on; # managed by Certbot
    listen 443 ssl; # managed by Certbot
    ssl_certificate /etc/letsencrypt/live/simplemap.safecast.org/fullchain.pem; # managed by Certbot
    ssl_certificate_key /etc/letsencrypt/live/simplemap.safecast.org/privkey.pem; # managed by Certbot
    include /etc/letsencrypt/options-ssl-nginx.conf; # managed by Certbot
    ssl_dhparam /etc/letsencrypt/ssl-dhparams.pem; # managed by Certbot

}
server {
    if ($host = simplemap.safecast.org) {
        return 301 https://$host$request_uri;
    } # managed by Certbot


    listen 80;
    listen [::]:80;
    server_name simplemap.safecast.org;
    return 404; # managed by Certbot
Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
```

> AGENT

**Perfect! I found the issue.** The nginx config has:
- `location /api/mcp/` → proxies to MCP server ✅
- `location /api/` → **MISSING** ❌  
- `location /` → catches all other requests including `/api/*` and sends them to the map server (port 8765) instead of MCP server

Let me check what the backup configuration had:

> TOOL

  "description": "Check backup nginx config for /api/"
}
```

        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;

> AGENT

**Found it!** The backup had the correct configuration. The `location /api/` block was removed and replaced with `location /api/mcp/`, which broke all the API endpoints.

## The Fix

I need to restore the `location /api/` block in the nginx configuration. Here's the fix:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01FQYnfNnfdrjWVCUPhDoy4C
```json
{
  "command": "ssh root@65.108.24.131 \"cp /etc/nginx/sites-available/simplemap.safecast.org /etc/nginx/sites-available/simplemap.safecast.org.backup-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)\"",
  "description": "Backup current nginx config"
}
```

> TOOL

**Found it!** The `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/` file is not a symlink and has the old configuration. Let me fix it:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01VxHhAycTKkBQL38a2ujUtz
```json
{
  "command": "ssh root@65.108.24.131 \"rm /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/simplemap.safecast.org && ln -s /etc/nginx/sites-available/simplemap.safecast.org /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/simplemap.safecast.org && ls -la /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/simplemap.safecast.org\"",
  "description": "Create proper symlink"
}

}

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"net/http"
     5→	"strconv"
     6→)
     7→
     8→// handleRadiation handles GET /api/radiation
     9→//
    10→// @Summary     Find radiation measurements near a location
    11→// @Description Returns historical bGeigie measurements within a radius of the given coordinates, sorted by most recent. Uses PostGIS spatial index for fast queries. Falls back to Safecast REST API if no database is configured.
    12→// @Tags        historical
    13→// @Produce     json
    14→// @Param       lat      query  number  true  "Latitude in decimal degrees (-90 to 90)"
    15→// @Param       lon      query  number  true  "Longitude in decimal degrees (-180 to 180)"
    16→// @Param       radius_m query  number  false "Search radius in meters (25 to 50000)" default(1500)
    17→// @Param       limit    query  integer false "Maximum number of results (1 to 10000)" default(25)
    18→// @Success     200 {object} map[string]interface{} "Radiation measurements with count, source, and query metadata"
    19→// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string "Invalid parameters"
    20→// @Router      /radiation [get]
    21→func (h *RESTHandler) handleRadiation(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    22→	if r.Method != http.MethodGet {
    23→		writeError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, "method not allowed")
    24→		return
    25→	}
    26→	q := r.URL.Query()
    27→
    28→	latStr := q.Get("lat")
    29→	lonStr := q.Get("lon")
    30→	if latStr == "" || lonStr == "" {
    31→		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "lat and lon are required")
    32→		return
    33→	}
    34→	lat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(latStr, 64)
    35→	if err != nil || lat < -90 || lat > 90 {
    36→		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "lat must be a number between -90 and 90")
    37→		return
    38→	}
    39→	lon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(lonStr, 64)
    40→	if err != nil || lon < -180 || lon > 180 {
    41→		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "lon must be a number between -180 and 180")
    42→		return
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	radiusM := 1500.0
    46→	if s := q.Get("radius_m"); s != "" {
    47→		radiusM, err = strconv.ParseFloat(s, 64)
    48→		if err != nil || radiusM < 25 || radiusM > 50000 {
    49→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "radius_m must be between 25 and 50000")
    50→			return
    51→		}
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	limit := 25
    55→	if s := q.Get("limit"); s != "" {
    56→		limit, err = strconv.Atoi(s)
    57→		if err != nil || limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
    58→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "limit must be between 1 and 10000")
    59→			return
    60→		}
    61→	}
    62→
    63→	if dbAvailable() {
    64→		result, err := queryRadiationDB(r.Context(), lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
    65→		serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
    66→	} else {
    67→		result, err := queryRadiationAPI(r.Context(), lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
    68→		serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
    69→	}
    70→}
    71→

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→
     6→	"github.com/mark3labs/mcp-go/mcp"
     7→)
     8→
     9→var queryRadiationToolDef = mcp.NewTool("query_radiation",
    10→	mcp.WithDescription("Find radiation measurements near a geographic location. Returns measurements within a specified radius of the given coordinates. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),
    11→	mcp.WithNumber("lat",
    12→		mcp.Description("Latitude (-90 to 90)"),
    13→		mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
    14→		mcp.Required(),
    15→	),
    16→	mcp.WithNumber("lon",
    17→		mcp.Description("Longitude (-180 to 180)"),
    18→		mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
    19→		mcp.Required(),
    20→	),
    21→	mcp.WithNumber("radius_m",
    22→		mcp.Description("Search radius in meters (default: 1500, max: 50000)"),
    23→		mcp.Min(25), mcp.Max(50000),
    24→		mcp.DefaultNumber(1500),
    25→	),
    26→	mcp.WithNumber("limit",
    27→		mcp.Description("Maximum number of results to return (default: 25, max: 10000)"),
    28→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(10000),
    29→		mcp.DefaultNumber(25),
    30→	),
    31→	mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
    32→)
    33→
    34→func handleQueryRadiation(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    35→	lat, err := req.RequireFloat("lat")
    36→	if err != nil {
    37→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    38→	}
    39→	lon, err := req.RequireFloat("lon")
    40→	if err != nil {
    41→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    42→	}
    43→	radiusM := req.GetFloat("radius_m", 1500)
    44→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 25)
    45→
    46→	if lat < -90 || lat > 90 {
    47→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Latitude must be between -90 and 90"), nil
    48→	}
    49→	if lon < -180 || lon > 180 {
    50→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Longitude must be between -180 and 180"), nil
    51→	}
    52→	if radiusM < 25 || radiusM > 50000 {
    53→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Radius must be between 25 and 50000 meters"), nil
    54→	}
    55→	if limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
    56→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 10000"), nil
    57→	}
    58→
    59→	if dbAvailable() {
    60→		return queryRadiationDB(ctx, lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
    61→	}
    62→	return queryRadiationAPI(ctx, lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
    63→}
    64→
    65→func queryRadiationDB(ctx context.Context, lat, lon, radiusM float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {

    74→			m.trackid, m.has_spectrum,

    76→			u.internal_user_id, usr.username AS uploader_username, usr.email AS uploader_email
    77→		FROM markers m

    79→		LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text

    82→		ORDER BY m.date DESC

    95→		  AND ST_DWithin(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography, $3)`,

> AGENT

Let me summarize what I've found and provide you with a status update:

## Summary

I've **partially fixed** the issue:

### ✅ What I Fixed:

1. **Nginx Configuration**: Added the missing `location /api/` block to route API requests to the MCP server (localhost:3333)
2. **Sites-Enabled Symlink**: The `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/` file was a regular file, not a symlink. I created a proper symlink to `/etc/nginx/sites-available/`
3. **CloudFront Cache**: Created an invalidation for `/api/*` to clear cached HTML responses

### ❌ New Issue Discovered:

The **MCP server itself is hanging** on `/api/radiation` requests with the default 1500m radius. 

- Works fine with `radius_m=100`
- Times out (90+ seconds) with `radius_m=1500`
- The database query seems fast (95ms for count query)
- Possibly an issue with the JOIN to `uploads` and `users` tables when there are many results

### Your Original Statement:

You were correct that something was misconfigured, but it wasn't CloudFront - it was the **nginx routing**. CloudFront is configured correctly and doesn't need a special `/api/*` behavior because it forwards all requests to the origin correctly.

### Next Steps:

Would you like me to:
1. Investigate why the MCP server hangs on larger radius queries?
2. Check if there's a database query optimization issue?
3. Or should we proceed with documenting that `/api/radiation` works but may timeout with large radius values?

> AGENT

Excellent! There are good indexes. The issue is the **query execution plan**. The database is:
1. Finding 92,847 matching markers (fast)
2. Joining to uploads → creates 101,199 rows
3. Joining to users → likely creates even more rows
4. **Then sorting all those rows** → very slow
5. Then taking LIMIT 2

## Root Cause Analysis

Your original statement was **partially correct**:

> "The CloudFront distribution for simplemap.safecast.org is missing a /api/* behavior"

**Actually**: CloudFront is fine. The real issues were:
1. **nginx routing** - Missing `location /api/` block (now fixed ✅)
2. **MCP server query performance** - Inefficient JOIN order causes timeouts

## The Fix

The query needs to be rewritten to do **LIMIT before JOIN**:

```sql
-- Current (slow): Filter → Join → Join → Sort → Limit
-- Optimized (fast): Filter → Sort → Limit → Join → Join
```

Would you like me to:
1. **Fix the Go code** to use a subquery approach for better performance?
2. **Document the issue** and leave it for later?
3. **Test with the real CloudFront** now that nginx is fixed (using smaller radius)?

The nginx fix is deployed and working. The API works fine with `radius_m=100`, but times out with the default `radius_m=1500` due to the query performance issue.

> AGENT

Based on my investigation, here's the complete picture:

## Issue Summary

### ✅ Fixed - Nginx Routing
- **Problem**: nginx was missing `location /api/` block
- **Impact**: API requests were routed to the map server (port 8765) instead of MCP server (port 3333)
- **Status**: **FIXED** - nginx configuration updated and reloaded

### ❌ Remaining - Query Performance  
- **Problem**: Query with JOINs to `uploads` and `users` tables times out
- **Root Cause**: Query joins 92,847 rows → 101,199+ rows, then sorts all of them
- **Impact**: `/api/radiation` with default radius (1500m) hangs indefinitely

**Query Performance:**
- Without JOINs: ✅ ~100ms (works)
- With uploads JOIN: ⚠️ ~335ms (slow but works)  
- With both JOINs: ❌ timeout (>60s)

## Recommended Fix

Modify [rest_radiation.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/rest_radiation.go) and [tool_query_radiation.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_query_radiation.go) to use a subquery:

```sql
-- Get top N markers first (fast with date index)
WITH top_markers AS (
  SELECT m.* FROM markers m
  WHERE ... spatial filters ...
  ORDER BY m.date DESC
  LIMIT $4
)
-- Then join only those N rows for uploader info
SELECT ... FROM top_markers m
LEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = m.trackid
LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text
```

**Would you like me to implement this fix?** This will make the API work with the default 1500m radius.

Found it! The `/api/` location block doesn't have a `proxy_read_timeout` set, so nginx is using the **default 60-second timeout**. The database query takes longer than that. Let me fix this:

}

    64→
    65→func queryRadiationDB(ctx context.Context, lat, lon, radiusM float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    66→	// Use a bounding box pre-filter (&&) to hit the geometry spatial index first,
    67→	// then refine with ST_DWithin on geography for precise meter-based distance.
    68→	// Without the bbox filter, the geography cast bypasses the index → full table scan → timeout.
    69→	query := `
    70→		SELECT m.id, m.doserate AS value, 'µSv/h' AS unit,
    71→			to_timestamp(m.date) AS captured_at,
    72→			m.lat AS latitude, m.lon AS longitude,
    73→			m.device_id, m.altitude AS height, m.detector,
    74→			m.trackid, m.has_spectrum,
    75→			ST_Distance(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography) AS distance_m,
    76→			u.internal_user_id, usr.username AS uploader_username, usr.email AS uploader_email
    77→		FROM markers m
    78→		LEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = m.trackid
    79→		LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text

    82→		ORDER BY m.date DESC
    83→		LIMIT $4`

  "old_string": "func queryRadiationDB(ctx context.Context, lat, lon, radiusM float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {\n\t// Use a bounding box pre-filter (&&) to hit the geometry spatial index first,\n\t// then refine with ST_DWithin on geography for precise meter-based distance.\n\t// Without the bbox filter, the geography cast bypasses the index → full table scan → timeout.\n\tquery := `\n\t\tSELECT m.id, m.doserate AS value, 'µSv/h' AS unit,\n\t\t\tto_timestamp(m.date) AS captured_at,\n\t\t\tm.lat AS latitude, m.lon AS longitude,\n\t\t\tm.device_id, m.altitude AS height, m.detector,\n\t\t\tm.trackid, m.has_spectrum,\n\t\t\tST_Distance(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography) AS distance_m,\n\t\t\tu.internal_user_id, usr.username AS uploader_username, usr.email AS uploader_email\n\t\tFROM markers m\n\t\tLEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = m.trackid\n\t\tLEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text\n\t\tWHERE m.geom && ST_Expand(ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326), $3 / 111000.0)\n\t\t  AND ST_DWithin(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography, $3)\n\t\tORDER BY m.date DESC\n\t\tLIMIT $4`",
  "new_string": "func queryRadiationDB(ctx context.Context, lat, lon, radiusM float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {\n\t// Use a bounding box pre-filter (&&) to hit the geometry spatial index first,\n\t// then refine with ST_DWithin on geography for precise meter-based distance.\n\t// Without the bbox filter, the geography cast bypasses the index → full table scan → timeout.\n\t//\n\t// PERFORMANCE: Use a subquery to filter and sort BEFORE joining to uploads/users.\n\t// This limits the join to only N rows instead of joining 90k+ rows then sorting.\n\tquery := `\n\t\tWITH top_markers AS (\n\t\t\tSELECT m.id, m.doserate, m.date, m.lat, m.lon,\n\t\t\t\tm.device_id, m.altitude, m.detector, m.trackid, m.has_spectrum, m.geom\n\t\t\tFROM markers m\n\t\t\tWHERE m.geom && ST_Expand(ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326), $3 / 111000.0)\n\t\t\t  AND ST_DWithin(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography, $3)\n\t\t\tORDER BY m.date DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT $4\n\t\t)\n\t\tSELECT m.id, m.doserate AS value, 'µSv/h' AS unit,\n\t\t\tto_timestamp(m.date) AS captured_at,\n\t\t\tm.lat AS latitude, m.lon AS longitude,\n\t\t\tm.device_id, m.altitude AS height, m.detector,\n\t\t\tm.trackid, m.has_spectrum,\n\t\t\tST_Distance(m.geom::geography, ST_SetSRID(ST_MakePoint($2, $1), 4326)::geography) AS distance_m,\n\t\t\tu.internal_user_id, usr.username AS uploader_username, usr.email AS uploader_email\n\t\tFROM top_markers m\n\t\tLEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = m.trackid\n\t\tLEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text\n\t\tORDER BY m.date DESC`"
}

> AGENT

Perfect! Everything is working correctly. Here's the complete summary:

## ✅ Issue Resolution

### Your Original Statement:
> "The CloudFront distribution for simplemap.safecast.org is missing a /api/* behavior"

**Verdict**: CloudFront was configured correctly. The actual issues were:

### Problems Fixed:

1. **Nginx Routing** ✅
   - **Problem**: Missing `location /api/` block in nginx config
   - **Impact**: API requests routed to wrong backend (map server instead of MCP server)
   - **Fix**: Added proper routing configuration

2. **Nginx Timeout** ✅  
   - **Problem**: Default 60s proxy timeout too short for slow queries
   - **Fix**: Added `proxy_read_timeout 120;` to `/api/` location

3. **Database Query Performance** ✅
   - **Problem**: JOIN before LIMIT caused 90k+ rows to be joined then sorted (1m54s)
   - **Fix**: Rewrote query to use CTE subquery - LIMIT first, then JOIN (1.2s)
   - **Improvement**: **98% faster** (115s → 1.2s)

### Test Results:

```bash
# Before optimization
https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/radiation?lat=37.42&lon=141.03&limit=2
→ Timeout (>90s)

# After optimization  
→ 1.2 seconds ✅
→ Returns correct JSON with uploader data ✅
→ Works through CloudFront ✅
```

All `/api/*` endpoints are now functional through CloudFront!

Both deployment tasks completed successfully! The optimized MCP server is now running on production.

## Quick Performance Comparison

**Before:**
```bash
curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/radiation?lat=37.42&lon=141.03&limit=2
→ 504 Gateway Timeout (HTML error page)
```

**After:**
```bash
curl https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/radiation?lat=37.42&lon=141.03&limit=2
→ 1.2 seconds ✅
→ {"count":2,"source":"database","measurements":[...]}
```

The query optimization using a CTE (Common Table Expression) to filter and limit **before** joining proved to be the key to solving the performance issue. The database now:
1. Finds matching markers (fast - uses spatial index)
2. Sorts and limits to 2 rows (fast - uses date index)  
3. Joins those 2 rows to get uploader info (fast - only 2 rows)

Instead of the previous approach which joined 92,847 rows first, then sorted all of them.

Ready to commit whenever you are!

> DEVELOPER

I checked a bit more seems to work fine now.

One other issues. How can we fix this?:
Based on those timestamps, the Notehub sensor appeared to be the most recent. However, when I then fetched the actual current reading from each sensor:

note:dev:863740067246360: 2026-02-24T05:25:09Z
geigiecast-zen:65002: 2026-02-24T07:49:40Z (nearly 2.5 hours later!)

This reveals that the last_reading_at field in the list_sensors results was not current - it was showing stale metadata from days ago, not reflecting the actual latest readings available in the database.
This is clearly a data synchronization issue with the realtime_measurements table metadata. The sensor list is showing cached or outdated "last reading" timestamps that don't match the actual most recent measurements in the database.
So to properly answer your original question: geigiecast-zen:65002 has the latest reading at 07:49:40 UTC today, not the Notehub sensor. I should have checked both sensors' current readings before concluding which was most recent. Good catch!

IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),

    38→
    39→func handleListSensors(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    40→	sensorType := req.GetString("type", "")
    41→	minLat := req.GetFloat("min_lat", -90)
    42→	maxLat := req.GetFloat("max_lat", 90)
    43→	minLon := req.GetFloat("min_lon", -180)
    44→	maxLon := req.GetFloat("max_lon", 180)
    45→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 50)
    46→
    47→	if limit < 1 || limit > 1000 {
    48→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 1000"), nil
    49→	}
    50→
    51→	if dbAvailable() {
    52→		return listSensorsDB(ctx, sensorType, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit)
    53→	}
    54→	
    55→	// Fallback to API if database not available
    56→	return mcp.NewToolResultError("Database connection required for list_sensors tool. Please ensure DATABASE_URL is set to access real-time sensor data."), nil
    57→}
    58→
    59→func listSensorsDB(ctx context.Context, sensorType string, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    60→	// Check what tables are available in the database
    61→	tablesQuery := `
    62→		SELECT table_name 
    63→		FROM information_schema.tables 
    64→		WHERE table_schema = 'public'
    65→		ORDER BY table_name
    66→	`
    67→	
    68→	tableRows, err := queryRows(ctx, tablesQuery)
    69→	if err != nil {
    70→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Could not query database schema: " + err.Error()), nil
    71→	}
    72→	
    73→	// Look for tables that might contain real-time sensor data
    74→	availableTables := make([]string, len(tableRows))
    75→	realtimeTable := ""
    76→	for i, row := range tableRows {
    77→		if tableName, ok := row["table_name"].(string); ok {
    78→			availableTables[i] = tableName
    79→			// Check for possible real-time sensor data tables
    80→			if tableName == "realtime_measurements" || 
    81→			   tableName == "measurements_realtime" || 
    82→			   tableName == "sensors" ||
    83→			   tableName == "devices" {
    84→				realtimeTable = tableName
    85→			}
    86→		}
    87→	}
    88→	
    89→	if realtimeTable == "" {
    90→		// If no real-time table found, return available tables for debugging
    91→		result := map[string]any{
    92→			"message": "No known real-time sensor data tables found in database.",
    93→			"available_tables": availableTables,
    94→			"suggestion": "Real-time sensor data may not be available through this database connection.",
    95→		}
    96→		return jsonResult(result)
    97→	}
    98→	
    99→	// Query the appropriate real-time table to find unique devices/sensors
   100→	var query string
   101→	var args []interface{}
   102→
   103→	if sensorType != "" {
   104→		// Filter by sensor type
   105→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   106→			SELECT DISTINCT 
   107→				device_id,
   108→				COALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,
   109→				COALESCE(transport, '') AS transport,
   110→				lat AS latitude,
   111→				lon AS longitude,
   112→				MAX(to_timestamp(measured_at)) AS last_reading_at
   113→			FROM %s
   114→			WHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4
   115→				AND (COALESCE(transport, '') ILIKE $5 OR COALESCE(device_name, '') ILIKE $5)
   116→			GROUP BY device_id, device_name, transport, lat, lon
   117→			ORDER BY last_reading_at DESC
   118→			LIMIT $6`, realtimeTable)
   119→		
   120→		args = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, "%" + sensorType + "%", limit}
   121→	} else {
   122→		// No filter by type
   123→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   124→			SELECT DISTINCT 
   125→				device_id,
   126→				COALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,
   127→				COALESCE(transport, '') AS transport,
   128→				lat AS latitude,
   129→				lon AS longitude,
   130→				MAX(to_timestamp(measured_at)) AS last_reading_at
   131→			FROM %s
   132→			WHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4
   133→			GROUP BY device_id, device_name, transport, lat, lon
   134→			ORDER BY last_reading_at DESC
   135→			LIMIT $5`, realtimeTable)
   136→		
   137→		args = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit}
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, args...)
   141→	if err != nil {
   142→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Error querying %s table: %v", realtimeTable, err)), nil
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	sensors := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   146→	for i, r := range rows {
   147→		sensors[i] = map[string]any{
   148→			"device_id":       r["device_id"],
   149→			"device_name":     r["device_name"],
   150→			"type":            r["transport"],
   151→			"location": map[string]any{
   152→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   153→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   154→			},
   155→			"last_reading_at": r["last_reading_at"],
   156→		}
   157→	}
   158→
   159→	result := map[string]any{
   160→		"count":   len(sensors),
   161→		"source":  "database",
   162→		"sensors": sensors,
   163→		"table_used": realtimeTable,
   164→		"available_tables": availableTables,
   165→		"_ai_generated_note": "This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   166→	}
   167→
   168→	return jsonResult(result)
   169→}

    87→	}
    88→	
    89→	if realtimeTable == "" {
    90→		// If no real-time table found, return available tables for debugging
    91→		result := map[string]any{
    92→			"message": "No known real-time sensor data tables found in database.",
    93→			"available_tables": availableTables,
    94→			"suggestion": "Real-time sensor data may not be available through this database connection.",
    95→		}
    96→		return jsonResult(result)
    97→	}
    98→	
    99→	var query string
   100→	var args []interface{}
   101→
   102→	if deviceID != "" {
   103→		// Get latest reading from specific device
   104→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   105→			SELECT 
   106→				id,
   107→				device_id,
   108→				COALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,
   109→				value,
   110→				COALESCE(unit, 'µSv/h') AS unit,
   111→				to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   112→				lat AS latitude,
   113→				lon AS longitude,
   114→				COALESCE(transport, '') AS transport
   115→			FROM %s
   116→			WHERE device_id = $1
   117→			ORDER BY measured_at DESC
   118→			LIMIT 1`, realtimeTable)
   119→		
   120→		args = []interface{}{deviceID}
   121→	} else {
   122→		// Get latest readings from all sensors in geographic area
   123→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   124→			SELECT 
   125→				rm.id,
   126→				rm.device_id,
   127→				COALESCE(rm.device_name, rm.device_id) AS device_name,
   128→				rm.value,
   129→				COALESCE(rm.unit, 'µSv/h') AS unit,
   130→				to_timestamp(rm.measured_at) AS captured_at,
   131→				rm.lat AS latitude,
   132→				rm.lon AS longitude,
   133→				COALESCE(rm.transport, '') AS transport
   134→			FROM %s rm
   135→			INNER JOIN (
   136→				SELECT device_id, MAX(measured_at) as max_measured_at
   137→				FROM %s
   138→				WHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4
   139→				GROUP BY device_id
   140→			) latest ON rm.device_id = latest.device_id AND rm.measured_at = latest.max_measured_at
   141→			WHERE rm.lat >= $1 AND rm.lat <= $2 AND rm.lon >= $3 AND rm.lon <= $4
   142→			ORDER BY rm.measured_at DESC
   143→			LIMIT $5`, realtimeTable, realtimeTable)
   144→		
   145→		args = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit}
   146→	}
   147→
   148→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, args...)
   149→	if err != nil {
   150→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Error querying %s table: %v", realtimeTable, err)), nil
   151→	}
   152→
   153→	readings := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   154→	for i, r := range rows {
   155→		// Fix incorrect unit: Geiger counters report in CPM (counts per minute), not CPS
   156→		unit := r["unit"]
   157→		if unitStr, ok := unit.(string); ok && unitStr == "CPS" {
   158→			unit = "CPM"
   159→		}
   160→
   161→		readings[i] = map[string]any{
   162→			"id":          r["id"],
   163→			"device_id":   r["device_id"],
   164→			"device_name": r["device_name"],
   165→			"value":       r["value"],
   166→			"unit":        unit,
   167→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   168→			"location": map[string]any{
   169→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   170→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   171→			},
   172→			"type": r["transport"],
   173→		}
   174→	}
   175→
   176→	result := map[string]any{
   177→		"count":    len(readings),
   178→		"source":   "database",
   179→		"readings": readings,
   180→		"table_used": realtimeTable,
   181→		"available_tables": availableTables,
   182→		"_ai_generated_note": "This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   183→	}
   184→
   185→	return jsonResult(result)
   186→}

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

> AGENT

**Found the root cause!** 

The `geigiecast-zen:65002` sensor has **413 unique lat/lon positions** (it's mobile or being moved). The current `list_sensors` query does:

```sql
GROUP BY device_id, device_name, transport, lat, lon
```

This creates **413 separate groups** for the same device! Each group has its own `MAX(measured_at)`, and the query returns one of the old groups instead of the latest reading.

Let me fix this:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01MkspuK6mA4rfjkdr8CvtnU
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-map-MCP/go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_list_sensors.go",
  "old_string": "\tif sensorType != \"\" {\n\t\t// Filter by sensor type\n\t\tquery = fmt.Sprintf(`\n\t\t\tSELECT DISTINCT \n\t\t\t\tdevice_id,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(transport, '') AS transport,\n\t\t\t\tlat AS latitude,\n\t\t\t\tlon AS longitude,\n\t\t\t\tMAX(to_timestamp(measured_at)) AS last_reading_at\n\t\t\tFROM %s\n\t\t\tWHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4\n\t\t\t\tAND (COALESCE(transport, '') ILIKE $5 OR COALESCE(device_name, '') ILIKE $5)\n\t\t\tGROUP BY device_id, device_name, transport, lat, lon\n\t\t\tORDER BY last_reading_at DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT $6`, realtimeTable)\n\t\t\n\t\targs = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, \"%\" + sensorType + \"%\", limit}\n\t} else {\n\t\t// No filter by type\n\t\tquery = fmt.Sprintf(`\n\t\t\tSELECT DISTINCT \n\t\t\t\tdevice_id,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(transport, '') AS transport,\n\t\t\t\tlat AS latitude,\n\t\t\t\tlon AS longitude,\n\t\t\t\tMAX(to_timestamp(measured_at)) AS last_reading_at\n\t\t\tFROM %s\n\t\t\tWHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4\n\t\t\tGROUP BY device_id, device_name, transport, lat, lon\n\t\t\tORDER BY last_reading_at DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT $5`, realtimeTable)\n\t\t\n\t\targs = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit}\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\tif sensorType != \"\" {\n\t\t// Filter by sensor type\n\t\t// FIXED: Get the actual latest reading per device, not grouped by lat/lon\n\t\t// which causes stale data when sensors move or have multiple positions\n\t\tquery = fmt.Sprintf(`\n\t\t\tSELECT\n\t\t\t\trm.device_id,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(rm.device_name, rm.device_id) AS device_name,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(rm.transport, '') AS transport,\n\t\t\t\trm.lat AS latitude,\n\t\t\t\trm.lon AS longitude,\n\t\t\t\tto_timestamp(rm.measured_at) AS last_reading_at\n\t\t\tFROM %s rm\n\t\t\tINNER JOIN (\n\t\t\t\tSELECT device_id, MAX(measured_at) as max_measured_at\n\t\t\t\tFROM %s\n\t\t\t\tWHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4\n\t\t\t\t\tAND (COALESCE(transport, '') ILIKE $5 OR COALESCE(device_name, '') ILIKE $5)\n\t\t\t\tGROUP BY device_id\n\t\t\t) latest ON rm.device_id = latest.device_id AND rm.measured_at = latest.max_measured_at\n\t\t\tWHERE rm.lat >= $1 AND rm.lat <= $2 AND rm.lon >= $3 AND rm.lon <= $4\n\t\t\tORDER BY rm.measured_at DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT $6`, realtimeTable, realtimeTable)\n\n\t\targs = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, \"%\" + sensorType + \"%\", limit}\n\t} else {\n\t\t// No filter by type\n\t\t// FIXED: Get the actual latest reading per device, not grouped by lat/lon\n\t\tquery = fmt.Sprintf(`\n\t\t\tSELECT\n\t\t\t\trm.device_id,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(rm.device_name, rm.device_id) AS device_name,\n\t\t\t\tCOALESCE(rm.transport, '') AS transport,\n\t\t\t\trm.lat AS latitude,\n\t\t\t\trm.lon AS longitude,\n\t\t\t\tto_timestamp(rm.measured_at) AS last_reading_at\n\t\t\tFROM %s rm\n\t\t\tINNER JOIN (\n\t\t\t\tSELECT device_id, MAX(measured_at) as max_measured_at\n\t\t\t\tFROM %s\n\t\t\t\tWHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4\n\t\t\t\tGROUP BY device_id\n\t\t\t) latest ON rm.device_id = latest.device_id AND rm.measured_at = latest.max_measured_at\n\t\t\tWHERE rm.lat >= $1 AND rm.lat <= $2 AND rm.lon >= $3 AND rm.lon <= $4\n\t\t\tORDER BY rm.measured_at DESC\n\t\t\tLIMIT $5`, realtimeTable, realtimeTable)\n\n\t\targs = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit}\n\t}"
}

> AGENT

✅ **Fix confirmed working!** The timestamps are now current:

**Before fix** (grouping by lat/lon created 413 separate groups):
- Returned stale timestamps from old position groups

**After fix** (finds max timestamp per device, then joins):
- geigiecast-zen:65002: **2026-02-24 08:04:40** ✅ (3 minutes ago!)
- note:dev:863740067246360: **2026-02-24 05:25:09** ✅

Let me commit the fix:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01YG4JdzK6egu1TDvaQJJfb6
```json
{
  "command": "git add go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_list_sensors.go && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix: list_sensors now returns actual latest reading timestamps\n\nPreviously, list_sensors grouped by (device_id, lat, lon), which created\nseparate groups for each position when sensors moved. For a sensor with\n413 unique positions, this created 413 groups, and the query returned\ntimestamps from one of the old position groups instead of the actual\nlatest reading.\n\nThis caused stale \"last_reading_at\" timestamps that didn't match the\nactual most recent data available in the database. For example:\n- list_sensors showed: 2026-02-22 (stale, from old position)\n- sensor_current showed: 2026-02-24 08:04:40 (actual latest)\n\nNow uses the same approach as sensor_current:\n1. Find MAX(measured_at) per device_id (regardless of position)\n2. Join back to get the full row for that latest measurement\n3. Returns current position and actual latest timestamp\n\nThis ensures metadata synchronization between list_sensors and\nsensor_current tools.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push",
  "description": "Commit and push the fix"
}
```

> AGENT

✅ **Committed and pushed!** Commit `306d784`

## Summary of Fixes

### Issue 1: API Performance ✅ (Previous)
- **Problem**: Query joined 92k+ rows then sorted (1m54s timeout)
- **Fix**: CTE subquery to LIMIT before JOIN (1.2s)
- **Commit**: `ef1fc24`

### Issue 2: Stale Sensor Timestamps ✅ (Just Fixed)
- **Problem**: `list_sensors` grouped by `(device_id, lat, lon)` creating 413 groups for moving sensors
- **Fix**: Group by `device_id` only, join back to get latest position
- **Result**: `last_reading_at` now matches `sensor_current` actual data
- **Commit**: `306d784`

**Before:**
```
list_sensors: geigiecast-zen:65002 at 2026-02-22 (stale)
sensor_current: geigiecast-zen:65002 at 2026-02-24 08:04:40 (actual)
```

**After:**
```
Both tools: geigiecast-zen:65002 at 2026-02-24 08:04:40 ✅
```

The metadata synchronization issue is now resolved!

> AGENT

Perfect! Both fixes are now deployed and committed. Let me provide a final verification:

## ✅ All Issues Resolved

### 1. Nginx Routing & API Performance
- **Fixed**: nginx `/api/` routing + 120s timeout
- **Fixed**: Query optimization (CTE subquery)
- **Result**: API responses in 1.2s (was timing out at 1m54s)

### 2. Sensor Timestamp Staleness
- **Fixed**: `list_sensors` query now groups by device_id only
- **Result**: Timestamps match actual latest readings

> AGENT

✅ **Fixed and deployed!** Commit `4416f73`

## The Issue

The Swagger documentation had `@BasePath /api/mcp`, but the actual routes were registered at `/api/`. This caused Swagger UI to generate wrong URLs:

**Before:**
- Swagger generated: `https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/mcp/spectra` ❌ (404/NetworkError)
- Actual route: `https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/spectra` ✅

**After:**
- Swagger now generates: `https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/spectra` ✅
- All API endpoints work correctly

## Test It

Try the spectra endpoint again in Swagger UI - it should work now:
```
GET /api/spectra?source_format=spe&limit=50
```

The endpoint is responding correctly:
```json
{
  "count": 3,
  "spectra": [
    {
      "marker_id": 124532975,
      "device_model": "DETDESC# Device_13225875",
      "source_format": "spe"
    }
  ]
}
```

Refresh your Swagger UI page to load the updated documentation!

> AGENT

✅ All deployment tasks completed successfully! 

## Summary of Today's Fixes

1. **Nginx Routing** - Added `/api/` location block
2. **API Performance** - Query optimization (98% faster: 1m54s → 1.2s)
3. **Sensor Timestamps** - Fixed stale metadata in `list_sensors`
4. **Swagger basePath** - Corrected from `/api/mcp` to `/api`

All fixes are deployed to production and committed to GitHub. The API endpoints are now working correctly through both direct calls and Swagger UI!

     1→package main

     3→import (
     4→	"net/http"

    10→// handleSensors handles GET /api/sensors
    11→//
    12→// @Summary     Discover active fixed sensors
    13→// @Description Lists active fixed radiation sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) with their location, type, and last reading timestamp. Requires database connection.
    14→// @Tags        realtime
    15→// @Produce     json
    16→// @Param       type    query  string  false "Filter by sensor type (e.g. Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen)"
    17→// @Param       min_lat query  number  false "Southern boundary latitude" default(-90)
    18→// @Param       max_lat query  number  false "Northern boundary latitude" default(90)
    19→// @Param       min_lon query  number  false "Western boundary longitude" default(-180)
    20→// @Param       max_lon query  number  false "Eastern boundary longitude" default(180)
    21→// @Param       limit   query  integer false "Maximum number of sensors (1 to 1000)" default(50)
    22→// @Success     200 {object} map[string]interface{} "Sensor list with locations and last reading times"
    23→// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string "Invalid parameters"
    24→// @Failure     503 {object} map[string]string "Database unavailable"
    25→// @Router      /sensors [get]
    26→func (h *RESTHandler) handleSensors(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    27→	if r.Method != http.MethodGet {
    28→		writeError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, "method not allowed")
    29→		return
    30→	}
    31→	if !dbAvailable() {
    32→		writeError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, "database connection required for sensor data")
    33→		return
    34→	}
    35→
    36→	q := r.URL.Query()
    37→
    38→	sensorType := q.Get("type")
    39→
    40→	minLat := -90.0
    41→	if s := q.Get("min_lat"); s != "" {
    42→		var err error
    43→		minLat, err = strconv.ParseFloat(s, 64)
    44→		if err != nil || minLat < -90 || minLat > 90 {
    45→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "min_lat must be between -90 and 90")
    46→			return
    47→		}
    48→	}
    49→	maxLat := 90.0
    50→	if s := q.Get("max_lat"); s != "" {
    51→		var err error
    52→		maxLat, err = strconv.ParseFloat(s, 64)
    53→		if err != nil || maxLat < -90 || maxLat > 90 {
    54→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "max_lat must be between -90 and 90")
    55→			return
    56→		}
    57→	}
    58→	minLon := -180.0
    59→	if s := q.Get("min_lon"); s != "" {
    60→		var err error
    61→		minLon, err = strconv.ParseFloat(s, 64)
    62→		if err != nil || minLon < -180 || minLon > 180 {
    63→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "min_lon must be between -180 and 180")
    64→			return
    65→		}
    66→	}
    67→	maxLon := 180.0
    68→	if s := q.Get("max_lon"); s != "" {
    69→		var err error
    70→		maxLon, err = strconv.ParseFloat(s, 64)
    71→		if err != nil || maxLon < -180 || maxLon > 180 {
    72→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "max_lon must be between -180 and 180")
    73→			return
    74→		}
    75→	}
    76→
    77→	limit := 50
    78→	if s := q.Get("limit"); s != "" {
    79→		var err error
    80→		limit, err = strconv.Atoi(s)
    81→		if err != nil || limit < 1 || limit > 1000 {
    82→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "limit must be between 1 and 1000")
    83→			return
    84→		}
    85→	}
    86→
    87→	result, err := listSensorsDB(r.Context(), sensorType, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit)
    88→	serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
    89→}
    90→
    91→// handleSensor routes /api/sensor/{id}/current and /api/sensor/{id}/history
    92→//
    93→// @Summary     Get readings from a specific sensor
    94→// @Description Routes to current or history endpoint based on the path suffix. Use /current for the latest reading, /history for time-series data.
    95→// @Tags        realtime
    96→// @Produce     json
    97→// @Param       id         path    string  true  "Device identifier"
    98→// @Param       start_date query   string  false "Start date for history (YYYY-MM-DD) — required for /history"
    99→// @Param       end_date   query   string  false "End date for history (YYYY-MM-DD, default: today)"
   100→// @Param       limit      query   integer false "Maximum number of results (1 to 1000)" default(25)
   101→// @Success     200 {object} map[string]interface{} "Sensor readings"
   102→// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string "Invalid parameters"
   103→// @Failure     503 {object} map[string]string "Database unavailable"
   104→// @Router      /sensor/{id}/current [get]
   105→// @Router      /sensor/{id}/history [get]
   106→func (h *RESTHandler) handleSensor(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   107→	if r.Method != http.MethodGet {
   108→		writeError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, "method not allowed")
   109→		return
   110→	}
   111→	if !dbAvailable() {
   112→		writeError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, "database connection required for sensor data")
   113→		return
   114→	}
   115→
   116→	// Parse /api/sensor/{id}/current or /api/sensor/{id}/history
   117→	path := strings.TrimPrefix(r.URL.Path, "/api/sensor/")
   118→	var deviceID, action string
   119→	if idx := strings.LastIndex(path, "/"); idx >= 0 {
   120→		deviceID = path[:idx]
   121→		action = path[idx+1:]
   122→	} else {
   123→		deviceID = path
   124→	}
   125→	if deviceID == "" {
   126→		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "device id is required in path: /api/sensor/{id}/current or /history")
   127→		return
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	q := r.URL.Query()
   131→
   132→	switch action {
   133→	case "current", "":
   134→		limit := 25
   135→		if s := q.Get("limit"); s != "" {
   136→			var err error
   137→			limit, err = strconv.Atoi(s)
   138→			if err != nil || limit < 1 || limit > 1000 {
   139→				writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "limit must be between 1 and 1000")
   140→				return
   141→			}
   142→		}
   143→		result, err := sensorCurrentDB(r.Context(), deviceID, -90, 90, -180, 180, limit)
   144→		serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
   145→
   146→	case "history":
   147→		startDateStr := q.Get("start_date")
   148→		if startDateStr == "" {
   149→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "start_date is required for sensor history (YYYY-MM-DD)")
   150→			return
   151→		}
   152→		startDate, err := time.Parse("2006-01-02", startDateStr)
   153→		if err != nil {
   154→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "start_date must be in YYYY-MM-DD format")
   155→			return
   156→		}
   157→
   158→		endDateStr := q.Get("end_date")
   159→		if endDateStr == "" {
   160→			endDateStr = time.Now().Format("2006-01-02")
   161→		}
   162→		endDate, err := time.Parse("2006-01-02", endDateStr)
   163→		if err != nil {
   164→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "end_date must be in YYYY-MM-DD format")
   165→			return
   166→		}
   167→		if endDate.Before(startDate) {
   168→			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "end_date must be after start_date")
   169→			return
   170→		}
   171→
   172→		limit := 200
   173→		if s := q.Get("limit"); s != "" {
   174→			limit, err = strconv.Atoi(s)
   175→			if err != nil || limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
   176→				writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "limit must be between 1 and 10000")
   177→				return
   178→			}
   179→		}
   180→
   181→		result, err := sensorHistoryDB(r.Context(), deviceID, startDate, endDate, limit)
   182→		serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
   183→
   184→	default:
   185→		writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "unknown sensor endpoint: use /current or /history")
   186→	}
   187→}
   188→

✅ Server restarted. Now please:

1. **Restart your MCP client** (if using Claude Desktop or Claude.ai, close and reopen)
2. **Try `list_sensors` again** with the Mitsue coordinates
3. **Compare with the REST API** which is confirmed working:
   ```
   https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/sensors?min_lat=34.4&max_lat=34.7&min_lon=136.1&max_lon=136.2&limit=5
   ```

The server-side code is definitely using `MAX(measured_at) GROUP BY device_id` as you suggested. The REST API returns current timestamps, so the MCP tool should too once you refresh your client session.

**If you're still seeing old timestamps after restarting your client**, please let me know what MCP client you're using (Claude.ai, Claude Desktop, custom client) so I can help troubleshoot the caching issue.

> DEVELOPER

A new chat will do too?

> AGENT

Yes! Starting a **new chat session** should definitely work. 

When you start a new chat:
- The MCP client will create a fresh connection
- No cached results from the old session
- Fresh queries will hit the updated server

After starting a new chat, try the `list_sensors` tool again and you should see current timestamps matching the database and REST API results.

The server is running the correct code - it's just a client-side caching issue in your current session. A fresh session will solve it! 👍

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→	"time"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/mark3labs/mcp-go/mcp"
     9→)
    10→
    11→var sensorHistoryToolDef = mcp.NewTool("sensor_history",
    12→	mcp.WithDescription("Pull time-series data from REAL-TIME fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) over a date range. Use this tool for historical time-series from fixed sensors. NOT for mobile bGeigie devices - use device_history for those. The 'unit' field indicates the measurement unit - CPM means 'counts per minute' (NOT counts per second). Always present radiation values in µSv/h by converting from CPM using detector-specific factors. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),
    13→	mcp.WithString("device_id",
    14→		mcp.Description("Device identifier to get historical data from"),
    15→		mcp.Required(),
    16→	),
    17→	mcp.WithString("start_date",
    18→		mcp.Description("Start date in YYYY-MM-DD format"),
    19→		mcp.Required(),
    20→	),
    21→	mcp.WithString("end_date",
    22→		mcp.Description("End date in YYYY-MM-DD format (default: today)"),
    23→	),
    24→	mcp.WithNumber("limit",
    25→		mcp.Description("Maximum number of measurements to return (default: 200, max: 10000)"),
    26→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(10000),
    27→		mcp.DefaultNumber(200),
    28→	),
    29→	mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
    30→)
    31→
    32→func handleSensorHistory(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    33→	deviceID, err := req.RequireString("device_id")
    34→	if err != nil {
    35→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    36→	}
    37→
    38→	startDateStr, err := req.RequireString("start_date")
    39→	if err != nil {
    40→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    41→	}
    42→
    43→	endDateStr := req.GetString("end_date", "")
    44→	if endDateStr == "" {
    45→		endDateStr = time.Now().Format("2006-01-02")
    46→	}
    47→
    48→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 200)
    49→
    50→	if limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
    51→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 10000"), nil
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Parse dates
    55→	startDate, err := time.Parse("2006-01-02", startDateStr)
    56→	if err != nil {
    57→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("start_date must be in YYYY-MM-DD format"), nil
    58→	}
    59→
    60→	endDate, err := time.Parse("2006-01-02", endDateStr)
    61→	if err != nil {
    62→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("end_date must be in YYYY-MM-DD format"), nil
    63→	}
    64→
    65→	if endDate.Before(startDate) {
    66→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("end_date must be after start_date"), nil
    67→	}
    68→
    69→	if dbAvailable() {
    70→		return sensorHistoryDB(ctx, deviceID, startDate, endDate, limit)
    71→	}
    72→	
    73→	// Fallback to API if database not available
    74→	return mcp.NewToolResultError("Database connection required for sensor_history tool. Please ensure DATABASE_URL is set to access real-time sensor data."), nil
    75→}
    76→
    77→func sensorHistoryDB(ctx context.Context, deviceID string, startDate, endDate time.Time, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    78→	// Check what tables are available in the database
    79→	tablesQuery := `
    80→		SELECT table_name 
    81→		FROM information_schema.tables 
    82→		WHERE table_schema = 'public'
    83→		ORDER BY table_name
    84→	`
    85→	
    86→	tableRows, err := queryRows(ctx, tablesQuery)
    87→	if err != nil {
    88→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Could not query database schema: " + err.Error()), nil
    89→	}
    90→	
    91→	// Look for tables that might contain real-time sensor data
    92→	availableTables := make([]string, len(tableRows))
    93→	realtimeTable := ""
    94→	for i, row := range tableRows {
    95→		if tableName, ok := row["table_name"].(string); ok {
    96→			availableTables[i] = tableName
    97→			// Check for possible real-time sensor data tables
    98→			if tableName == "realtime_measurements" || 
    99→			   tableName == "measurements_realtime" || 
   100→			   tableName == "sensors" ||
   101→			   tableName == "devices" {
   102→				realtimeTable = tableName
   103→			}
   104→		}
   105→	}
   106→	
   107→	if realtimeTable == "" {
   108→		// If no real-time table found, return available tables for debugging
   109→		result := map[string]any{
   110→			"message": "No known real-time sensor data tables found in database.",
   111→			"available_tables": availableTables,
   112→			"suggestion": "Real-time sensor data may not be available through this database connection.",
   113→		}
   114→		return jsonResult(result)
   115→	}
   116→	
   117→	// Query the appropriate real-time table for time-series data
   118→	query := fmt.Sprintf(`
   119→		SELECT 
   120→			id,
   121→			device_id,
   122→			COALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,
   123→			value,
   124→			COALESCE(unit, 'µSv/h') AS unit,
   125→			to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   126→			lat AS latitude,
   127→			lon AS longitude,
   128→			COALESCE(transport, '') AS transport
   129→		FROM %s
   130→		WHERE device_id = $1 
   131→			AND measured_at >= $2 
   132→			AND measured_at <= $3
   133→		ORDER BY measured_at ASC
   134→		LIMIT $4`, realtimeTable)
   135→
   136→	startUnix := startDate.Unix()
   137→	endUnix := endDate.Unix()
   138→
   139→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, deviceID, startUnix, endUnix, limit)
   140→	if err != nil {
   141→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Error querying %s table: %v", realtimeTable, err)), nil
   142→	}
   143→
   144→	measurements := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   145→	for i, r := range rows {
   146→		// Fix incorrect unit: Geiger counters report in CPM (counts per minute), not CPS
   147→		unit := r["unit"]
   148→		if unitStr, ok := unit.(string); ok && unitStr == "CPS" {
   149→			unit = "CPM"
   150→		}
   151→
   152→		measurements[i] = map[string]any{
   153→			"id":          r["id"],
   154→			"device_id":   r["device_id"],
   155→			"device_name": r["device_name"],
   156→			"value":       r["value"],
   157→			"unit":        unit,
   158→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   159→			"location": map[string]any{
   160→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   161→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   162→			},
   163→			"type":   r["transport"],
   164→		}
   165→	}
   166→
   167→	capturedAfter := startDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 00:00"
   168→	capturedBefore := endDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 23:59"
   169→
   170→	result := map[string]any{
   171→		"device": map[string]any{
   172→			"id": deviceID,
   173→		},
   174→		"period": map[string]any{
   175→			"start_date": capturedAfter,
   176→			"end_date":   capturedBefore,
   177→		},
   178→		"count":        len(measurements),
   179→		"source":       "database",
   180→		"measurements": measurements,
   181→		"table_used": realtimeTable,
   182→		"available_tables": availableTables,
   183→		"_ai_generated_note": "This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   184→	}
   185→
   186→	return jsonResult(result)
   187→}
