     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"time"
     6→
     7→	"github.com/mark3labs/mcp-go/mcp"
     8→)
     9→
    10→var deviceHistoryToolDef = mcp.NewTool("device_history",
    11→	mcp.WithDescription("Get historical measurements from MOBILE bGeigie survey devices or historical import data. Use this tool ONLY for mobile devices, NOT for real-time fixed sensors (use sensor_current/sensor_history for those). Radiation values are typically in CPM (counts per minute, NOT counts per second). Always present radiation values in µSv/h by converting from CPM using detector-specific factors. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),
    12→	mcp.WithString("device_id",
    13→		mcp.Description("Device identifier"),
    14→		mcp.Required(),
    15→	),
    16→	mcp.WithNumber("days",
    17→		mcp.Description("Number of days of history to retrieve (default: 30, max: 365)"),
    18→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(365),
    19→		mcp.DefaultNumber(30),
    20→	),
    21→	mcp.WithNumber("limit",
    22→		mcp.Description("Maximum number of measurements to return (default: 200, max: 10000)"),
    23→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(10000),
    24→		mcp.DefaultNumber(200),
    25→	),
    26→	mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
    27→)
    28→
    29→func handleDeviceHistory(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    30→	deviceIDStr, err := req.RequireString("device_id")
    31→	if err != nil {
    32→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    33→	}
    34→
    35→	days := req.GetInt("days", 30)
    36→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 200)
    37→
    38→	if days < 1 || days > 365 {
    39→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("days must be between 1 and 365"), nil
    40→	}
    41→	if limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
    42→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 10000"), nil
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	if dbAvailable() {
    46→		return deviceHistoryDB(ctx, deviceIDStr, days, limit)
    47→	}
    48→	return deviceHistoryAPI(ctx, deviceIDStr, days, limit)
    49→}
    50→
    51→func deviceHistoryDB(ctx context.Context, deviceID string, days, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    52→	now := time.Now().UTC()
    53→	startDate := now.AddDate(0, 0, -days)
    54→
    55→	// Query both markers table and realtime_measurements table
    56→	// First, try markers table (bGeigie imports)
    57→	markersQuery := `
    58→		SELECT m.id, m.doserate AS value, 'µSv/h' AS unit,
    59→			to_timestamp(m.date) AS captured_at,
    60→			m.lat AS latitude, m.lon AS longitude,
    61→			m.altitude AS height, m.detector, m.trackid::text AS track_id,
    62→			u.internal_user_id, usr.username AS uploader_username, usr.email AS uploader_email
    63→		FROM markers m
    64→		LEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = m.trackid
    65→		LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text
    66→		WHERE m.device_id = $1 AND m.date >= $2 AND m.date <= $3
    67→		ORDER BY m.date DESC
    68→		LIMIT $4`
    69→
    70→	markersRows, err := queryRows(ctx, markersQuery, deviceID, startDate.Unix(), now.Unix(), limit)
    71→	if err != nil {
    72→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	// Then, query realtime_measurements table (fixed sensors)
    76→	// First, check if the table exists and what columns it has
    77→	columnsQuery := `
    78→		SELECT column_name
    79→		FROM information_schema.columns
    80→		WHERE table_name = 'realtime_measurements'
    81→		ORDER BY column_name
    82→	`
    83→	
    84→	var realtimeRows []map[string]any
    85→	
    86→	columnRows, err := queryRows(ctx, columnsQuery)
    87→	if err != nil || len(columnRows) == 0 {
    88→		// If we can't query the schema or table doesn't exist, try the basic query
    89→		realtimeQuery := `
    90→			SELECT id, value, unit,
    91→				to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
    92→				lat AS latitude, lon AS longitude,
    93→				device_name, transport, device_id
    94→			FROM realtime_measurements
    95→			WHERE device_id = $1 AND measured_at >= $2 AND measured_at <= $3
    96→			ORDER BY measured_at DESC
    97→			LIMIT $4`
    98→
    99→		realtimeRows, err = queryRows(ctx, realtimeQuery, deviceID, startDate.Unix(), now.Unix(), limit)
   100→		if err != nil {
   101→			return mcp.NewToolResultError("Error querying realtime_measurements table: " + err.Error()), nil
   102→		}
   103→	} else {
   104→		// Build the query based on available columns
   105→		hasHeight := false
   106→		for _, row := range columnRows {
   107→			if colName, ok := row["column_name"].(string); ok && colName == "height" {
   108→				hasHeight = true
   109→				break
   110→			}
   111→		}
   112→		
   113→		var realtimeQuery string
   114→		if hasHeight {
   115→			realtimeQuery = `
   116→				SELECT id, value, unit,
   117→					to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   118→					lat AS latitude, lon AS longitude,
   119→					device_name, transport, device_id, height
   120→				FROM realtime_measurements
   121→				WHERE device_id = $1 AND measured_at >= $2 AND measured_at <= $3
   122→				ORDER BY measured_at DESC
   123→				LIMIT $4`
   124→		} else {
   125→			realtimeQuery = `
   126→				SELECT id, value, unit,
   127→					to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   128→					lat AS latitude, lon AS longitude,
   129→					device_name, transport, device_id
   130→				FROM realtime_measurements
   131→				WHERE device_id = $1 AND measured_at >= $2 AND measured_at <= $3
   132→				ORDER BY measured_at DESC
   133→				LIMIT $4`
   134→		}
   135→
   136→		realtimeRows, err = queryRows(ctx, realtimeQuery, deviceID, startDate.Unix(), now.Unix(), limit)
   137→		if err != nil {
   138→			return mcp.NewToolResultError("Error querying realtime_measurements table: " + err.Error()), nil
   139→		}
   140→	}
   141→
   142→	// Combine results and sort by timestamp (most recent first)
   143→	allMeasurements := make([]map[string]any, 0, len(markersRows)+len(realtimeRows))
   144→	
   145→	// Process markers results
   146→	for _, r := range markersRows {
   147→		measurement := map[string]any{
   148→			"id":    r["id"],
   149→			"value": r["value"],
   150→			"unit":  r["unit"],
   151→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   152→			"location": map[string]any{
   153→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   154→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   155→			},
   156→			"height":   r["height"],
   157→			"detector": r["detector"],
   158→			"source":   "bgeigie_import",
   159→		}
   160→		if r["track_id"] != nil {
   161→			measurement["track_id"] = r["track_id"]
   162→		}
   163→
   164→		// Add uploader information if available
   165→		if uploaderUsername, ok := r["uploader_username"]; ok && uploaderUsername != nil && uploaderUsername != "" {
   166→			measurement["uploader"] = map[string]any{
   167→				"username": uploaderUsername,
   168→				"email":    r["uploader_email"],
   169→			}
   170→		}
   171→
   172→		allMeasurements = append(allMeasurements, measurement)
   173→	}
   174→	
   175→	// Process realtime results
   176→	for _, r := range realtimeRows {
   177→		// Fix incorrect unit: Geiger counters report in CPM (counts per minute), not CPS
   178→		unit := r["unit"]
   179→		if unitStr, ok := unit.(string); ok && unitStr == "CPS" {
   180→			unit = "CPM"
   181→		}
   182→
   183→		measurement := map[string]any{
   184→			"id":    r["id"],
   185→			"value": r["value"],
   186→			"unit":  unit,
   187→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   188→			"location": map[string]any{
   189→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   190→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   191→			},
   192→			"height":   r["height"],
   193→			"device_name": r["device_name"],
   194→			"type":     r["transport"],
   195→			"source":   "realtime_sensor",
   196→		}
   197→		allMeasurements = append(allMeasurements, measurement)
   198→	}
   199→
   200→	// Sort all measurements by captured_at timestamp (most recent first)
   201→	// Since queryRows returns timestamps as interface{}, we need to handle both string and time.Time types
   202→	for i := 0; i < len(allMeasurements)-1; i++ {
   203→		for j := 0; j < len(allMeasurements)-i-1; j++ {
   204→			// Get the timestamp values - they could be strings or time.Time objects
   205→			time1Val := allMeasurements[j]["captured_at"]
   206→			time2Val := allMeasurements[j+1]["captured_at"]
   207→			
   208→			// Compare timestamps - swap if j-th element is older than (j+1)-th element
   209→			shouldSwap := false
   210→			
   211→			// Handle different possible types for timestamps
   212→			switch v1 := time1Val.(type) {
   213→			case string:
   214→				if v2, ok := time2Val.(string); ok {
   215→					if v1 < v2 {
   216→						shouldSwap = true
   217→					}
   218→				}
   219→			case time.Time:
   220→				if v2, ok := time2Val.(time.Time); ok {
   221→					if v1.Before(v2) {
   222→						shouldSwap = true
   223→					}
   224→				}
   225→			}
   226→			
   227→			if shouldSwap {
   228→				allMeasurements[j], allMeasurements[j+1] = allMeasurements[j+1], allMeasurements[j]
   229→			}
   230→		}
   231→	}
   232→
   233→	// Apply limit
   234→	measurements := allMeasurements
   235→	if len(measurements) > limit {
   236→		measurements = measurements[:limit]
   237→	}
   238→
   239→	capturedAfter := startDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 00:00"
   240→	capturedBefore := now.Format("2006-01-02") + " 23:59"
   241→
   242→	result := map[string]any{
   243→		"device": map[string]any{
   244→			"id": deviceID,
   245→		},
   246→		"period": map[string]any{
   247→			"days":       days,
   248→			"start_date": capturedAfter,
   249→			"end_date":   capturedBefore,
   250→		},
   251→		"count":        len(measurements),
   252→		"source":       "database",
   253→		"measurements": measurements,
   254→		"_ai_generated_note": "This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   255→	}
   256→
   257→	return jsonResult(result)
   258→}
   259→
   260→func deviceHistoryAPI(ctx context.Context, deviceIDStr string, days, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   261→	resp, err := client.GetRealtimeHistory(ctx, deviceIDStr)
   262→	if err != nil {
   263→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
   264→	}
   265→
   266→	now := time.Now().UTC()
   267→	startDate := now.AddDate(0, 0, -days)
   268→	capturedAfter := startDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 00:00"
   269→	capturedBefore := now.Format("2006-01-02") + " 23:59"
   270→	startUnix := startDate.Unix()
   271→
   272→	// Extract dose rate time series and filter by date range
   273→	var measurements []map[string]any
   274→	if series, ok := resp["series"].(map[string]any); ok {
   275→		if doseRate, ok := series["doseRate"].([]any); ok {
   276→			for _, raw := range doseRate {
   277→				pt, ok := raw.(map[string]any)
   278→				if !ok {
   279→					continue
   280→				}
   281→				ts, ok := toFloat(pt["time"])
   282→				if !ok || int64(ts) < startUnix {
   283→					continue
   284→				}
   285→				t := time.Unix(int64(ts), 0).UTC()
   286→				measurements = append(measurements, map[string]any{
   287→					"value":       pt["value"],
   288→					"unit":        "µSv/h",
   289→					"captured_at": t.Format(time.RFC3339),
   290→				})
   291→			}
   292→		}
   293→	}
   294→
   295→	totalAvailable := len(measurements)
   296→	if limit > len(measurements) {
   297→		limit = len(measurements)
   298→	}
   299→	measurements = measurements[:limit]
   300→
   301→	deviceInfo := map[string]any{
   302→		"id": deviceIDStr,
   303→	}
   304→	if name, ok := resp["deviceName"].(string); ok && name != "" {
   305→		deviceInfo["name"] = name
   306→	}
   307→	if tube, ok := resp["tube"].(string); ok && tube != "" {
   308→		deviceInfo["sensor"] = tube
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	result := map[string]any{
   312→		"device": deviceInfo,
   313→		"period": map[string]any{
   314→			"days":       days,
   315→			"start_date": capturedAfter,
   316→			"end_date":   capturedBefore,
   317→		},
   318→		"count":           len(measurements),
   319→		"total_available": totalAvailable,
   320→		"source":          "api",
   321→		"measurements":    measurements,
   322→		"_ai_generated_note": "This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   323→	}
   324→
   325→	if ranges, ok := resp["ranges"].(map[string]any); ok {
   326→		if dr, ok := ranges["doseRate"].(map[string]any); ok {
   327→			result["statistics"] = map[string]any{
   328→				"min_usvh": dr["min"],
   329→				"max_usvh": dr["max"],
   330→				"avg_usvh": dr["avg"],
   331→			}
   332→		}
   333→	}
   334→
   335→	return jsonResult(result)
   336→}
   337→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

> AGENT

Perfect! The CPM/CPS confusion fix has been successfully deployed. Here's what I did:

## Changes Made

Enhanced the `_ai_generated_note` field in all sensor-related MCP tools to explicitly warn about the CPM vs CPS distinction:

**Files Updated:**
- [tool_sensor_current.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_sensor_current.go#L182)
- [tool_sensor_history.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_sensor_history.go#L183)
- [tool_device_history.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_device_history.go#L254) (both DB and API functions)
- [tool_list_sensors.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_list_sensors.go#L178)

**New Warning Message:**
```
IMPORTANT: When reporting radiation values in CPM, always state 'CPM (counts per minute)', 
NOT 'CPS (counts per second)'. CPM is the standard unit for Geiger counter measurements.
```

This warning will now appear in every response from these tools, helping prevent AI assistants (like Claude in other sessions) from making the CPM/CPS mistake when interpreting Safecast radiation data.

## Deployment Status

✅ Code committed: [`03cff98`](https://github.com/Safecast/safecast-map-MCP/commit/03cff98)  
✅ Pushed to GitHub  
✅ Built and deployed to simplemap.safecast.org  
✅ Service running with checksum verified  
✅ API tested - new warning message appearing in responses

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→
     7→	"github.com/mark3labs/mcp-go/mcp"
     8→)
     9→
    10→var sensorCurrentToolDef = mcp.NewTool("sensor_current",
    11→	mcp.WithDescription("Get the latest reading(s) from REAL-TIME fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.). Use this tool when users ask about 'current', 'latest', 'live', or 'real-time' sensor data. NOT for mobile bGeigie devices - use device_history for those. The 'unit' field indicates the measurement unit - CPM means 'counts per minute' (NOT counts per second). Always present radiation values in µSv/h by converting from CPM using detector-specific factors. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),
    12→	mcp.WithString("device_id",
    13→		mcp.Description("Specific device ID to get latest reading from"),
    14→	),
    15→	mcp.WithNumber("min_lat",
    16→		mcp.Description("Southern boundary for geographic filter"),
    17→		mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
    18→	),
    19→	mcp.WithNumber("max_lat",
    20→		mcp.Description("Northern boundary for geographic filter"),
    21→		mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
    22→	),
    23→	mcp.WithNumber("min_lon",
    24→		mcp.Description("Western boundary for geographic filter"),
    25→		mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
    26→	),
    27→	mcp.WithNumber("max_lon",
    28→		mcp.Description("Eastern boundary for geographic filter"),
    29→		mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
    30→	),
    31→	mcp.WithNumber("limit",
    32→		mcp.Description("Maximum number of readings to return (default: 25, max: 1000)"),
    33→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(1000),
    34→		mcp.DefaultNumber(25),
    35→	),
    36→	mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
    37→)
    38→
    39→func handleSensorCurrent(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    40→	deviceID := req.GetString("device_id", "")
    41→	minLat := req.GetFloat("min_lat", -90)
    42→	maxLat := req.GetFloat("max_lat", 90)
    43→	minLon := req.GetFloat("min_lon", -180)
    44→	maxLon := req.GetFloat("max_lon", 180)
    45→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 25)
    46→
    47→	if limit < 1 || limit > 1000 {
    48→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 1000"), nil
    49→	}
    50→
    51→	if dbAvailable() {
    52→		return sensorCurrentDB(ctx, deviceID, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit)
    53→	}
    54→	
    55→	// Fallback to API if database not available
    56→	return mcp.NewToolResultError("Database connection required for sensor_current tool. Please ensure DATABASE_URL is set to access real-time sensor data."), nil
    57→}
    58→
    59→func sensorCurrentDB(ctx context.Context, deviceID string, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    60→	// Check what tables are available in the database
    61→	tablesQuery := `
    62→		SELECT table_name 
    63→		FROM information_schema.tables 
    64→		WHERE table_schema = 'public'
    65→		ORDER BY table_name
    66→	`
    67→	
    68→	tableRows, err := queryRows(ctx, tablesQuery)
    69→	if err != nil {
    70→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Could not query database schema: " + err.Error()), nil
    71→	}
    72→	
    73→	// Look for tables that might contain real-time sensor data
    74→	availableTables := make([]string, len(tableRows))
    75→	realtimeTable := ""
    76→	for i, row := range tableRows {
    77→		if tableName, ok := row["table_name"].(string); ok {
    78→			availableTables[i] = tableName
    79→			// Check for possible real-time sensor data tables
    80→			if tableName == "realtime_measurements" || 
    81→			   tableName == "measurements_realtime" || 
    82→			   tableName == "sensors" ||
    83→			   tableName == "devices" {
    84→				realtimeTable = tableName
    85→			}
    86→		}
    87→	}
    88→	
    89→	if realtimeTable == "" {
    90→		// If no real-time table found, return available tables for debugging
    91→		result := map[string]any{
    92→			"message": "No known real-time sensor data tables found in database.",
    93→			"available_tables": availableTables,
    94→			"suggestion": "Real-time sensor data may not be available through this database connection.",
    95→		}
    96→		return jsonResult(result)
    97→	}
    98→	
    99→	var query string
   100→	var args []interface{}
   101→
   102→	if deviceID != "" {
   103→		// Get latest reading from specific device
   104→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   105→			SELECT 
   106→				id,
   107→				device_id,
   108→				COALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,
   109→				value,
   110→				COALESCE(unit, 'µSv/h') AS unit,
   111→				to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   112→				lat AS latitude,
   113→				lon AS longitude,
   114→				COALESCE(transport, '') AS transport
   115→			FROM %s
   116→			WHERE device_id = $1
   117→			ORDER BY measured_at DESC
   118→			LIMIT 1`, realtimeTable)
   119→		
   120→		args = []interface{}{deviceID}
   121→	} else {
   122→		// Get latest readings from all sensors in geographic area
   123→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   124→			SELECT 
   125→				rm.id,
   126→				rm.device_id,
   127→				COALESCE(rm.device_name, rm.device_id) AS device_name,
   128→				rm.value,
   129→				COALESCE(rm.unit, 'µSv/h') AS unit,
   130→				to_timestamp(rm.measured_at) AS captured_at,
   131→				rm.lat AS latitude,
   132→				rm.lon AS longitude,
   133→				COALESCE(rm.transport, '') AS transport
   134→			FROM %s rm
   135→			INNER JOIN (
   136→				SELECT device_id, MAX(measured_at) as max_measured_at
   137→				FROM %s
   138→				WHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4
   139→				GROUP BY device_id
   140→			) latest ON rm.device_id = latest.device_id AND rm.measured_at = latest.max_measured_at
   141→			WHERE rm.lat >= $1 AND rm.lat <= $2 AND rm.lon >= $3 AND rm.lon <= $4
   142→			ORDER BY rm.measured_at DESC
   143→			LIMIT $5`, realtimeTable, realtimeTable)
   144→		
   145→		args = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit}
   146→	}
   147→
   148→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, args...)
   149→	if err != nil {
   150→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Error querying %s table: %v", realtimeTable, err)), nil
   151→	}
   152→
   153→	readings := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   154→	for i, r := range rows {
   155→		// Fix incorrect unit: Geiger counters report in CPM (counts per minute), not CPS
   156→		unit := r["unit"]
   157→		if unitStr, ok := unit.(string); ok && unitStr == "CPS" {
   158→			unit = "CPM"
   159→		}
   160→
   161→		readings[i] = map[string]any{
   162→			"id":          r["id"],
   163→			"device_id":   r["device_id"],
   164→			"device_name": r["device_name"],
   165→			"value":       r["value"],
   166→			"unit":        unit,
   167→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   168→			"location": map[string]any{
   169→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   170→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   171→			},
   172→			"type": r["transport"],
   173→		}
   174→	}
   175→
   176→	result := map[string]any{
   177→		"count":    len(readings),
   178→		"source":   "database",
   179→		"readings": readings,
   180→		"table_used": realtimeTable,
   181→		"available_tables": availableTables,
   182→		"_ai_generated_note": "IMPORTANT: When reporting radiation values in CPM, always state 'CPM (counts per minute)', NOT 'CPS (counts per second)'. CPM is the standard unit for Geiger counter measurements. This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   183→	}
   184→
   185→	return jsonResult(result)
   186→}

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→	"time"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/mark3labs/mcp-go/mcp"
     9→)
    10→
    11→var sensorHistoryToolDef = mcp.NewTool("sensor_history",
    12→	mcp.WithDescription("Pull time-series data from REAL-TIME fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) over a date range. Use this tool for historical time-series from fixed sensors. NOT for mobile bGeigie devices - use device_history for those. The 'unit' field indicates the measurement unit - CPM means 'counts per minute' (NOT counts per second). Always present radiation values in µSv/h by converting from CPM using detector-specific factors. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),
    13→	mcp.WithString("device_id",
    14→		mcp.Description("Device identifier to get historical data from"),
    15→		mcp.Required(),
    16→	),
    17→	mcp.WithString("start_date",
    18→		mcp.Description("Start date in YYYY-MM-DD format"),
    19→		mcp.Required(),
    20→	),
    21→	mcp.WithString("end_date",
    22→		mcp.Description("End date in YYYY-MM-DD format (default: today)"),
    23→	),
    24→	mcp.WithNumber("limit",
    25→		mcp.Description("Maximum number of measurements to return (default: 200, max: 10000)"),
    26→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(10000),
    27→		mcp.DefaultNumber(200),
    28→	),
    29→	mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
    30→)
    31→
    32→func handleSensorHistory(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    33→	deviceID, err := req.RequireString("device_id")
    34→	if err != nil {
    35→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    36→	}
    37→
    38→	startDateStr, err := req.RequireString("start_date")
    39→	if err != nil {
    40→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    41→	}
    42→
    43→	endDateStr := req.GetString("end_date", "")
    44→	if endDateStr == "" {
    45→		endDateStr = time.Now().Format("2006-01-02")
    46→	}
    47→
    48→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 200)
    49→
    50→	if limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
    51→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 10000"), nil
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Parse dates
    55→	startDate, err := time.Parse("2006-01-02", startDateStr)
    56→	if err != nil {
    57→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("start_date must be in YYYY-MM-DD format"), nil
    58→	}
    59→
    60→	endDate, err := time.Parse("2006-01-02", endDateStr)
    61→	if err != nil {
    62→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("end_date must be in YYYY-MM-DD format"), nil
    63→	}
    64→
    65→	if endDate.Before(startDate) {
    66→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("end_date must be after start_date"), nil
    67→	}
    68→
    69→	if dbAvailable() {
    70→		return sensorHistoryDB(ctx, deviceID, startDate, endDate, limit)
    71→	}
    72→	
    73→	// Fallback to API if database not available
    74→	return mcp.NewToolResultError("Database connection required for sensor_history tool. Please ensure DATABASE_URL is set to access real-time sensor data."), nil
    75→}
    76→
    77→func sensorHistoryDB(ctx context.Context, deviceID string, startDate, endDate time.Time, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    78→	// Check what tables are available in the database
    79→	tablesQuery := `
    80→		SELECT table_name 
    81→		FROM information_schema.tables 
    82→		WHERE table_schema = 'public'
    83→		ORDER BY table_name
    84→	`
    85→	
    86→	tableRows, err := queryRows(ctx, tablesQuery)
    87→	if err != nil {
    88→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Could not query database schema: " + err.Error()), nil
    89→	}
    90→	
    91→	// Look for tables that might contain real-time sensor data
    92→	availableTables := make([]string, len(tableRows))
    93→	realtimeTable := ""
    94→	for i, row := range tableRows {
    95→		if tableName, ok := row["table_name"].(string); ok {
    96→			availableTables[i] = tableName
    97→			// Check for possible real-time sensor data tables
    98→			if tableName == "realtime_measurements" || 
    99→			   tableName == "measurements_realtime" || 
   100→			   tableName == "sensors" ||
   101→			   tableName == "devices" {
   102→				realtimeTable = tableName
   103→			}
   104→		}
   105→	}
   106→	
   107→	if realtimeTable == "" {
   108→		// If no real-time table found, return available tables for debugging
   109→		result := map[string]any{
   110→			"message": "No known real-time sensor data tables found in database.",
   111→			"available_tables": availableTables,
   112→			"suggestion": "Real-time sensor data may not be available through this database connection.",
   113→		}
   114→		return jsonResult(result)
   115→	}
   116→	
   117→	// Query the appropriate real-time table for time-series data
   118→	query := fmt.Sprintf(`
   119→		SELECT 
   120→			id,
   121→			device_id,
   122→			COALESCE(device_name, device_id) AS device_name,
   123→			value,
   124→			COALESCE(unit, 'µSv/h') AS unit,
   125→			to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   126→			lat AS latitude,
   127→			lon AS longitude,
   128→			COALESCE(transport, '') AS transport
   129→		FROM %s
   130→		WHERE device_id = $1 
   131→			AND measured_at >= $2 
   132→			AND measured_at <= $3
   133→		ORDER BY measured_at ASC
   134→		LIMIT $4`, realtimeTable)
   135→
   136→	startUnix := startDate.Unix()
   137→	endUnix := endDate.Unix()
   138→
   139→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, deviceID, startUnix, endUnix, limit)
   140→	if err != nil {
   141→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Error querying %s table: %v", realtimeTable, err)), nil
   142→	}
   143→
   144→	measurements := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   145→	for i, r := range rows {
   146→		// Fix incorrect unit: Geiger counters report in CPM (counts per minute), not CPS
   147→		unit := r["unit"]
   148→		if unitStr, ok := unit.(string); ok && unitStr == "CPS" {
   149→			unit = "CPM"
   150→		}
   151→
   152→		measurements[i] = map[string]any{
   153→			"id":          r["id"],
   154→			"device_id":   r["device_id"],
   155→			"device_name": r["device_name"],
   156→			"value":       r["value"],
   157→			"unit":        unit,
   158→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   159→			"location": map[string]any{
   160→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   161→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   162→			},
   163→			"type":   r["transport"],
   164→		}
   165→	}
   166→
   167→	capturedAfter := startDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 00:00"
   168→	capturedBefore := endDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 23:59"
   169→
   170→	result := map[string]any{
   171→		"device": map[string]any{
   172→			"id": deviceID,
   173→		},
   174→		"period": map[string]any{
   175→			"start_date": capturedAfter,
   176→			"end_date":   capturedBefore,
   177→		},
   178→		"count":        len(measurements),
   179→		"source":       "database",
   180→		"measurements": measurements,
   181→		"table_used": realtimeTable,
   182→		"available_tables": availableTables,
   183→		"_ai_generated_note": "IMPORTANT: When reporting radiation values in CPM, always state 'CPM (counts per minute)', NOT 'CPS (counts per second)'. CPM is the standard unit for Geiger counter measurements. This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   184→	}
   185→
   186→	return jsonResult(result)
   187→}

    10→var deviceHistoryToolDef = mcp.NewTool("device_history",
    11→	mcp.WithDescription("Get historical measurements from MOBILE bGeigie survey devices or historical import data. Use this tool ONLY for mobile devices, NOT for real-time fixed sensors (use sensor_current/sensor_history for those). Radiation values are typically in CPM (counts per minute, NOT counts per second). Always present radiation values in µSv/h by converting from CPM using detector-specific factors. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),
    12→	mcp.WithString("device_id",
    13→		mcp.Description("Device identifier"),
    14→		mcp.Required(),
    15→	),
    16→	mcp.WithNumber("days",
    17→		mcp.Description("Number of days of history to retrieve (default: 30, max: 365)"),
    18→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(365),
    19→		mcp.DefaultNumber(30),
    20→	),

    29→func handleDeviceHistory(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    30→	deviceIDStr, err := req.RequireString("device_id")
    31→	if err != nil {
    32→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    33→	}
    34→
    35→	days := req.GetInt("days", 30)
    36→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 200)
    37→
    38→	if days < 1 || days > 365 {
    39→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("days must be between 1 and 365"), nil
    40→	}
    41→	if limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
    42→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 10000"), nil
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	if dbAvailable() {
    46→		return deviceHistoryDB(ctx, deviceIDStr, days, limit)
    47→	}
    48→	return deviceHistoryAPI(ctx, deviceIDStr, days, limit)
    49→}

    51→func deviceHistoryDB(ctx context.Context, deviceID string, days, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    52→	now := time.Now().UTC()
    53→	startDate := now.AddDate(0, 0, -days)
    54→
    55→	// Query both markers table and realtime_measurements table
    56→	// First, try markers table (bGeigie imports)
    57→	markersQuery := `
    58→		SELECT m.id, m.doserate AS value, 'µSv/h' AS unit,
    59→			to_timestamp(m.date) AS captured_at,
    60→			m.lat AS latitude, m.lon AS longitude,
    61→			m.altitude AS height, m.detector, m.trackid::text AS track_id,
    62→			u.internal_user_id, usr.username AS uploader_username, usr.email AS uploader_email
    63→		FROM markers m
    64→		LEFT JOIN uploads u ON u.track_id = m.trackid
    65→		LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text
    66→		WHERE m.device_id = $1 AND m.date >= $2 AND m.date <= $3
    67→		ORDER BY m.date DESC
    68→		LIMIT $4`
    69→
    70→	markersRows, err := queryRows(ctx, markersQuery, deviceID, startDate.Unix(), now.Unix(), limit)
    71→	if err != nil {
    72→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	// Then, query realtime_measurements table (fixed sensors)
    76→	// First, check if the table exists and what columns it has
    77→	columnsQuery := `
    78→		SELECT column_name
    79→		FROM information_schema.columns
    80→		WHERE table_name = 'realtime_measurements'
    81→		ORDER BY column_name
    82→	`
    83→	
    84→	var realtimeRows []map[string]any
    85→	
    86→	columnRows, err := queryRows(ctx, columnsQuery)
    87→	if err != nil || len(columnRows) == 0 {
    88→		// If we can't query the schema or table doesn't exist, try the basic query
    89→		realtimeQuery := `
    90→			SELECT id, value, unit,
    91→				to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
    92→				lat AS latitude, lon AS longitude,
    93→				device_name, transport, device_id
    94→			FROM realtime_measurements
    95→			WHERE device_id = $1 AND measured_at >= $2 AND measured_at <= $3
    96→			ORDER BY measured_at DESC
    97→			LIMIT $4`
    98→
    99→		realtimeRows, err = queryRows(ctx, realtimeQuery, deviceID, startDate.Unix(), now.Unix(), limit)
   100→		if err != nil {
   101→			return mcp.NewToolResultError("Error querying realtime_measurements table: " + err.Error()), nil
   102→		}
   103→	} else {
   104→		// Build the query based on available columns
   105→		hasHeight := false
   106→		for _, row := range columnRows {
   107→			if colName, ok := row["column_name"].(string); ok && colName == "height" {
   108→				hasHeight = true
   109→				break
   110→			}
   111→		}
   112→		
   113→		var realtimeQuery string
   114→		if hasHeight {
   115→			realtimeQuery = `
   116→				SELECT id, value, unit,
   117→					to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   118→					lat AS latitude, lon AS longitude,
   119→					device_name, transport, device_id, height
   120→				FROM realtime_measurements
   121→				WHERE device_id = $1 AND measured_at >= $2 AND measured_at <= $3
   122→				ORDER BY measured_at DESC
   123→				LIMIT $4`
   124→		} else {
   125→			realtimeQuery = `
   126→				SELECT id, value, unit,
   127→					to_timestamp(measured_at) AS captured_at,
   128→					lat AS latitude, lon AS longitude,
   129→					device_name, transport, device_id
   130→				FROM realtime_measurements
   131→				WHERE device_id = $1 AND measured_at >= $2 AND measured_at <= $3
   132→				ORDER BY measured_at DESC
   133→				LIMIT $4`
   134→		}
   135→
   136→		realtimeRows, err = queryRows(ctx, realtimeQuery, deviceID, startDate.Unix(), now.Unix(), limit)
   137→		if err != nil {
   138→			return mcp.NewToolResultError("Error querying realtime_measurements table: " + err.Error()), nil
   139→		}
   140→	}
   141→
   142→	// Combine results and sort by timestamp (most recent first)
   143→	allMeasurements := make([]map[string]any, 0, len(markersRows)+len(realtimeRows))
   144→	
   145→	// Process markers results
   146→	for _, r := range markersRows {
   147→		measurement := map[string]any{
   148→			"id":    r["id"],
   149→			"value": r["value"],
   150→			"unit":  r["unit"],
   151→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   152→			"location": map[string]any{
   153→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   154→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   155→			},
   156→			"height":   r["height"],
   157→			"detector": r["detector"],
   158→			"source":   "bgeigie_import",
   159→		}
   160→		if r["track_id"] != nil {
   161→			measurement["track_id"] = r["track_id"]
   162→		}
   163→
   164→		// Add uploader information if available
   165→		if uploaderUsername, ok := r["uploader_username"]; ok && uploaderUsername != nil && uploaderUsername != "" {
   166→			measurement["uploader"] = map[string]any{
   167→				"username": uploaderUsername,
   168→				"email":    r["uploader_email"],
   169→			}
   170→		}
   171→
   172→		allMeasurements = append(allMeasurements, measurement)
   173→	}
   174→	
   175→	// Process realtime results
   176→	for _, r := range realtimeRows {
   177→		// Fix incorrect unit: Geiger counters report in CPM (counts per minute), not CPS
   178→		unit := r["unit"]
   179→		if unitStr, ok := unit.(string); ok && unitStr == "CPS" {
   180→			unit = "CPM"
   181→		}
   182→
   183→		measurement := map[string]any{
   184→			"id":    r["id"],
   185→			"value": r["value"],
   186→			"unit":  unit,
   187→			"captured_at": r["captured_at"],
   188→			"location": map[string]any{
   189→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   190→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   191→			},
   192→			"height":   r["height"],
   193→			"device_name": r["device_name"],
   194→			"type":     r["transport"],
   195→			"source":   "realtime_sensor",
   196→		}
   197→		allMeasurements = append(allMeasurements, measurement)
   198→	}
   199→
   200→	// Sort all measurements by captured_at timestamp (most recent first)
   201→	// Since queryRows returns timestamps as interface{}, we need to handle both string and time.Time types
   202→	for i := 0; i < len(allMeasurements)-1; i++ {
   203→		for j := 0; j < len(allMeasurements)-i-1; j++ {
   204→			// Get the timestamp values - they could be strings or time.Time objects
   205→			time1Val := allMeasurements[j]["captured_at"]
   206→			time2Val := allMeasurements[j+1]["captured_at"]
   207→			
   208→			// Compare timestamps - swap if j-th element is older than (j+1)-th element
   209→			shouldSwap := false
   210→			
   211→			// Handle different possible types for timestamps
   212→			switch v1 := time1Val.(type) {
   213→			case string:
   214→				if v2, ok := time2Val.(string); ok {
   215→					if v1 < v2 {
   216→						shouldSwap = true
   217→					}
   218→				}
   219→			case time.Time:
   220→				if v2, ok := time2Val.(time.Time); ok {
   221→					if v1.Before(v2) {
   222→						shouldSwap = true
   223→					}
   224→				}
   225→			}
   226→			
   227→			if shouldSwap {
   228→				allMeasurements[j], allMeasurements[j+1] = allMeasurements[j+1], allMeasurements[j]
   229→			}
   230→		}
   231→	}
   232→
   233→	// Apply limit
   234→	measurements := allMeasurements
   235→	if len(measurements) > limit {
   236→		measurements = measurements[:limit]
   237→	}
   238→
   239→	capturedAfter := startDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 00:00"
   240→	capturedBefore := now.Format("2006-01-02") + " 23:59"
   241→
   242→	result := map[string]any{
   243→		"device": map[string]any{
   244→			"id": deviceID,
   245→		},
   246→		"period": map[string]any{
   247→			"days":       days,
   248→			"start_date": capturedAfter,
   249→			"end_date":   capturedBefore,
   250→		},
   251→		"count":        len(measurements),
   252→		"source":       "database",
   253→		"measurements": measurements,
   254→		"_ai_generated_note": "IMPORTANT: When reporting radiation values in CPM, always state 'CPM (counts per minute)', NOT 'CPS (counts per second)'. CPM is the standard unit for Geiger counter measurements. This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   255→	}
   256→
   257→	return jsonResult(result)
   258→}
   259→
   260→func deviceHistoryAPI(ctx context.Context, deviceIDStr string, days, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   261→	resp, err := client.GetRealtimeHistory(ctx, deviceIDStr)
   262→	if err != nil {
   263→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
   264→	}
   265→
   266→	now := time.Now().UTC()
   267→	startDate := now.AddDate(0, 0, -days)
   268→	capturedAfter := startDate.Format("2006-01-02") + " 00:00"
   269→	capturedBefore := now.Format("2006-01-02") + " 23:59"
   270→	startUnix := startDate.Unix()
   271→
   272→	// Extract dose rate time series and filter by date range
   273→	var measurements []map[string]any
   274→	if series, ok := resp["series"].(map[string]any); ok {
   275→		if doseRate, ok := series["doseRate"].([]any); ok {
   276→			for _, raw := range doseRate {
   277→				pt, ok := raw.(map[string]any)
   278→				if !ok {
   279→					continue
   280→				}
   281→				ts, ok := toFloat(pt["time"])
   282→				if !ok || int64(ts) < startUnix {
   283→					continue
   284→				}
   285→				t := time.Unix(int64(ts), 0).UTC()
   286→				measurements = append(measurements, map[string]any{
   287→					"value":       pt["value"],
   288→					"unit":        "µSv/h",
   289→					"captured_at": t.Format(time.RFC3339),
   290→				})
   291→			}
   292→		}
   293→	}
   294→
   295→	totalAvailable := len(measurements)
   296→	if limit > len(measurements) {
   297→		limit = len(measurements)
   298→	}
   299→	measurements = measurements[:limit]
   300→
   301→	deviceInfo := map[string]any{
   302→		"id": deviceIDStr,
   303→	}
   304→	if name, ok := resp["deviceName"].(string); ok && name != "" {
   305→		deviceInfo["name"] = name
   306→	}
   307→	if tube, ok := resp["tube"].(string); ok && tube != "" {
   308→		deviceInfo["sensor"] = tube
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	result := map[string]any{
   312→		"device": deviceInfo,
   313→		"period": map[string]any{
   314→			"days":       days,
   315→			"start_date": capturedAfter,
   316→			"end_date":   capturedBefore,
   317→		},
   318→		"count":           len(measurements),
   319→		"total_available": totalAvailable,
   320→		"source":          "api",
   321→		"measurements":    measurements,
   322→		"_ai_generated_note": "IMPORTANT: When reporting radiation values in CPM, always state 'CPM (counts per minute)', NOT 'CPS (counts per second)'. CPM is the standard unit for Geiger counter measurements. This data was retrieved by an AI assistant using Safecast tools. The interpretation and presentation of this data may be influenced by the AI system.",
   323→	}
   324→
   325→	if ranges, ok := resp["ranges"].(map[string]any); ok {
   326→		if dr, ok := ranges["doseRate"].(map[string]any); ok {
   327→			result["statistics"] = map[string]any{
   328→				"min_usvh": dr["min"],
   329→				"max_usvh": dr["max"],
   330→				"avg_usvh": dr["avg"],
   331→			}
   332→		}
   333→	}
   334→
   335→	return jsonResult(result)
   336→}
   337→

     1→package main

     3→import (
     4→	"context"

     9→
    10→var listSensorsToolDef = mcp.NewTool("list_sensors",
    11→	mcp.WithDescription("Discover active fixed sensors (Pointcast, Solarcast, bGeigieZen, etc.) by location or type, returning device IDs, locations, status, and last reading timestamp. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool."),
    12→	mcp.WithString("type",
    13→		mcp.Description("Filter by sensor type (e.g., 'Pointcast', 'Solarcast', 'bGeigieZen', etc.)"),
    14→	),
    15→	mcp.WithNumber("min_lat",
    16→		mcp.Description("Southern boundary for geographic filter"),
    17→		mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
    18→	),
    19→	mcp.WithNumber("max_lat",
    20→		mcp.Description("Northern boundary for geographic filter"),
    21→		mcp.Min(-90), mcp.Max(90),
    22→	),
    23→	mcp.WithNumber("min_lon",
    24→		mcp.Description("Western boundary for geographic filter"),
    25→		mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
    26→	),
    27→	mcp.WithNumber("max_lon",
    28→		mcp.Description("Eastern boundary for geographic filter"),
    29→		mcp.Min(-180), mcp.Max(180),
    30→	),
    31→	mcp.WithNumber("limit",
    32→		mcp.Description("Maximum number of sensors to return (default: 50, max: 1000)"),
    33→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(1000),
    34→		mcp.DefaultNumber(50),
    35→	),
    36→	mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),
    37→)
    38→
    39→func handleListSensors(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    40→	sensorType := req.GetString("type", "")
    41→	minLat := req.GetFloat("min_lat", -90)
    42→	maxLat := req.GetFloat("max_lat", 90)
    43→	minLon := req.GetFloat("min_lon", -180)
    44→	maxLon := req.GetFloat("max_lon", 180)
    45→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 50)
    46→
    47→	if limit < 1 || limit > 1000 {
    48→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 1000"), nil
    49→	}
    50→
    51→	if dbAvailable() {
    52→		return listSensorsDB(ctx, sensorType, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit)
    53→	}
    54→	
    55→	// Fallback to API if database not available
    56→	return mcp.NewToolResultError("Database connection required for list_sensors tool. Please ensure DATABASE_URL is set to access real-time sensor data."), nil
    57→}
    58→
    59→func listSensorsDB(ctx context.Context, sensorType string, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon float64, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    60→	// Check what tables are available in the database
    61→	tablesQuery := `
    62→		SELECT table_name 
    63→		FROM information_schema.tables 
    64→		WHERE table_schema = 'public'
    65→		ORDER BY table_name
    66→	`
    67→	
    68→	tableRows, err := queryRows(ctx, tablesQuery)
    69→	if err != nil {
    70→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Could not query database schema: " + err.Error()), nil
    71→	}
    72→	
    73→	// Look for tables that might contain real-time sensor data
    74→	availableTables := make([]string, len(tableRows))
    75→	realtimeTable := ""
    76→	for i, row := range tableRows {
    77→		if tableName, ok := row["table_name"].(string); ok {
    78→			availableTables[i] = tableName
    79→			// Check for possible real-time sensor data tables
    80→			if tableName == "realtime_measurements" || 
    81→			   tableName == "measurements_realtime" || 
    82→			   tableName == "sensors" ||
    83→			   tableName == "devices" {
    84→				realtimeTable = tableName
    85→			}
    86→		}
    87→	}
    88→	
    89→	if realtimeTable == "" {
    90→		// If no real-time table found, return available tables for debugging
    91→		result := map[string]any{
    92→			"message": "No known real-time sensor data tables found in database.",
    93→			"available_tables": availableTables,
    94→			"suggestion": "Real-time sensor data may not be available through this database connection.",
    95→		}
    96→		return jsonResult(result)
    97→	}
    98→	
    99→	// Query the appropriate real-time table to find unique devices/sensors
   100→	var query string
   101→	var args []interface{}
   102→
   103→	if sensorType != "" {
   104→		// Filter by sensor type
   105→		// FIXED: Get the actual latest reading per device, not grouped by lat/lon
   106→		// which causes stale data when sensors move or have multiple positions
   107→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   108→			SELECT
   109→				rm.device_id,
   110→				COALESCE(rm.device_name, rm.device_id) AS device_name,
   111→				COALESCE(rm.transport, '') AS transport,
   112→				rm.lat AS latitude,
   113→				rm.lon AS longitude,
   114→				to_timestamp(rm.measured_at) AS last_reading_at
   115→			FROM %s rm
   116→			INNER JOIN (
   117→				SELECT device_id, MAX(measured_at) as max_measured_at
   118→				FROM %s
   119→				WHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4
   120→					AND (COALESCE(transport, '') ILIKE $5 OR COALESCE(device_name, '') ILIKE $5)
   121→				GROUP BY device_id
   122→			) latest ON rm.device_id = latest.device_id AND rm.measured_at = latest.max_measured_at
   123→			WHERE rm.lat >= $1 AND rm.lat <= $2 AND rm.lon >= $3 AND rm.lon <= $4
   124→			ORDER BY rm.measured_at DESC
   125→			LIMIT $6`, realtimeTable, realtimeTable)
   126→
   127→		args = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, "%" + sensorType + "%", limit}
   128→	} else {
   129→		// No filter by type
   130→		// FIXED: Get the actual latest reading per device, not grouped by lat/lon
   131→		query = fmt.Sprintf(`
   132→			SELECT
   133→				rm.device_id,
   134→				COALESCE(rm.device_name, rm.device_id) AS device_name,
   135→				COALESCE(rm.transport, '') AS transport,
   136→				rm.lat AS latitude,
   137→				rm.lon AS longitude,
   138→				to_timestamp(rm.measured_at) AS last_reading_at
   139→			FROM %s rm
   140→			INNER JOIN (
   141→				SELECT device_id, MAX(measured_at) as max_measured_at
   142→				FROM %s
   143→				WHERE lat >= $1 AND lat <= $2 AND lon >= $3 AND lon <= $4
   144→				GROUP BY device_id
   145→			) latest ON rm.device_id = latest.device_id AND rm.measured_at = latest.max_measured_at
   146→			WHERE rm.lat >= $1 AND rm.lat <= $2 AND rm.lon >= $3 AND rm.lon <= $4
   147→			ORDER BY rm.measured_at DESC
   148→			LIMIT $5`, realtimeTable, realtimeTable)
   149→
   150→		args = []interface{}{minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon, limit}
   151→	}
   152→
   153→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, args...)
   154→	if err != nil {
   155→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(fmt.Sprintf("Error querying %s table: %v", realtimeTable, err)), nil
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	sensors := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   159→	for i, r := range rows {
   160→		sensors[i] = map[string]any{
   161→			"device_id":       r["device_id"],
   162→			"device_name":     r["device_name"],
   163→			"type":            r["transport"],
   164→			"location": map[string]any{
   165→				"latitude":  r["latitude"],
   166→				"longitude": r["longitude"],
   167→			},
   168→			"last_reading_at": r["last_reading_at"],

## Summary

✅ **Changed:** Moved the CPM/CPS warning from `_ai_generated_note` to a new `_ai_hint` field  
✅ **Benefit:** AI bots still get the critical CPM guidance, but user-facing messages are now cleaner  
✅ **Committed:** [`de4b411`](https://github.com/Safecast/safecast-map-MCP/commit/de4b411)  
✅ **Deployed:** Running on simplemap.safecast.org with checksum verified  

The `_ai_hint` field provides guidance to AI assistants without requiring it to be displayed to end users, while `_ai_generated_note` remains the user-facing disclaimer.

> AGENT

Found it! The issue is that the unit in the database is `"lnd_7318c_cps"` (lowercase, with detector model), not just `"CPS"`. Our conversion code only checks for exact match `"CPS"` so it never triggers. Let me fix this:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
id: toolu_0167bb95yH43cAji2TPGvoKo
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-map-MCP/go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_sensor_current.go",
  "offset": 150,
  "limit": 25
}
```

   151→	}

   153→	readings := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))
   154→	for i, r := range rows {
   155→		// Fix incorrect unit: Geiger counters report in CPM (counts per minute), not CPS
   156→		unit := r["unit"]
   157→		if unitStr, ok := unit.(string); ok && unitStr == "CPS" {

> AGENT

Looking at these three screenshots, I can see:

## Good News ✅
**CPM is now being reported correctly!**
- "38 CPM" (not CPS)
- "37 CPM" (not CPS)
- The unit conversion is working

## Still an Issue ❌
**Personal language persists:**
- "I'll search for the latest..."
- "Perfect! There it is!"
- "You're right - I see it now..."

Despite our `_ai_hint` saying "Present all data in a purely scientific, factual manner without personal pronouns (I, we), exclamations, or conversational phrases", the AI in that session is still using personal language.

## Why This Happens
The `_ai_hint` field provides guidance, but we can't fully control how other AI sessions interpret it. Different AI instances may prioritize user experience (conversational tone) over strict adherence to data formatting hints.

## Possible Solutions

Would you like me to:

1. **Make the hint even MORE explicit** - Add something like "NEVER use 'I', 'we', 'Perfect!', 'Great!', etc. Report ONLY raw facts."

2. **Add it to the tool description itself** (which AI sees before calling the tool) - This might have more influence

3. **Accept this limitation** - The CPM/CPS issue (the critical scientific accuracy problem) is now fixed. The conversational tone, while not ideal, doesn't affect data accuracy.

What would you prefer?

    12→var listTracksToolDef = mcp.NewTool("list_tracks",
    13→	mcp.WithDescription("Browse bGeigie Import tracks (bulk radiation measurement drives). Can filter by year, month, and detector/device name. IMPORTANT: Every response includes an _ai_generated_note field. You MUST display this note verbatim to the user in every response that uses data from this tool. When referencing or linking to track data, ALWAYS use https://simplemap.safecast.org as the base URL — NEVER use api.safecast.org, which does not host track data."),
    14→	mcp.WithNumber("year",
    15→		mcp.Description("Filter by year (e.g., 2024)"),
    16→		mcp.Min(2000), mcp.Max(2100),
    17→	),
    18→	mcp.WithNumber("month",
    19→		mcp.Description("Filter by month (1-12, requires year parameter)"),
    20→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(12),
    21→	),
    22→	mcp.WithString("detector",
    23→		mcp.Description("Filter by detector/device name (e.g., 'bGeigieZen', 'bGeigie', 'Pointcast'). Partial match supported."),
    24→	),
    25→	mcp.WithString("username",
    26→		mcp.Description("Filter by uploader username. Partial match supported."),
    27→	),
    28→	mcp.WithNumber("limit",
    29→		mcp.Description("Maximum number of results to return (default: 50, max: 50000)"),
    30→		mcp.Min(1), mcp.Max(50000),
    31→		mcp.DefaultNumber(50),
    32→	),
    33→	mcp.WithReadOnlyHintAnnotation(true),

    35→
    36→func handleListTracks(ctx context.Context, req mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    37→	year := req.GetInt("year", 0)
    38→	month := req.GetInt("month", 0)
    39→	detector := req.GetString("detector", "")
    40→	username := req.GetString("username", "")
    41→	limit := req.GetInt("limit", 50)
    42→
    43→	if month != 0 && year == 0 {
    44→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Month filter requires year parameter"), nil
    45→	}
    46→	if year != 0 && (year < 2000 || year > 2100) {
    47→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Year must be between 2000 and 2100"), nil
    48→	}
    49→	if month != 0 && (month < 1 || month > 12) {
    50→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Month must be between 1 and 12"), nil
    51→	}
    52→	if limit < 1 || limit > 50000 {
    53→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Limit must be between 1 and 50000"), nil
    54→	}
    55→
    56→	// DB is always preferred — the API fallback calls simplemap.safecast.org/api/tracks
    57→	// which is this server itself, causing infinite recursion.
    58→	if dbAvailable() {
    59→		return listTracksDB(ctx, year, month, detector, username, limit)
    60→	}
    61→
    62→	// DB unavailable and filters require it
    63→	if detector != "" || username != "" {
    64→		return mcp.NewToolResultError("Detector/username filtering requires database access"), nil
    65→	}
    66→
    67→	// No DB: fall back to the upstream simplemap API for older years only.
    68→	// This path only works correctly when the server is not the simplemap backend itself.
    69→	return listTracksAPI(ctx, year, month, limit)
    70→}
    71→
    72→func listTracksDB(ctx context.Context, year, month int, detector, username string, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
    73→	query := `SELECT u.id, u.filename, u.file_type, u.track_id, u.file_size,
    74→			u.created_at, u.source, u.source_id, u.recording_date,
    75→			u.detector, u.username,
    76→			u.internal_user_id, usr.username AS internal_username, usr.email AS uploader_email
    77→		FROM uploads u
    78→		LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text
    79→		WHERE 1=1`
    80→
    81→	args := []any{}
    82→	argIdx := 1
    83→
    84→	if year != 0 {
    85→		startDate := time.Date(year, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
    86→		endDate := time.Date(year+1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
    87→		if month != 0 {
    88→			startDate = time.Date(year, time.Month(month), 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
    89→			if month == 12 {
    90→				endDate = time.Date(year+1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
    91→			} else {
    92→				endDate = time.Date(year, time.Month(month+1), 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
    93→			}
    94→		}
    95→		query += fmt.Sprintf(" AND recording_date >= $%d AND recording_date < $%d", argIdx, argIdx+1)
    96→		args = append(args, startDate, endDate)
    97→		argIdx += 2
    98→	}
    99→
   100→	if detector != "" {
   101→		query += fmt.Sprintf(" AND detector ILIKE $%d", argIdx)
   102→		args = append(args, "%"+detector+"%")
   103→		argIdx++
   104→	}
   105→
   106→	if username != "" {
   107→		query += fmt.Sprintf(" AND (u.username ILIKE $%d OR usr.username ILIKE $%d OR usr.email ILIKE $%d)", argIdx, argIdx, argIdx)
   108→		args = append(args, "%"+username+"%")
   109→		argIdx++
   110→	}
   111→
   112→	query += " ORDER BY recording_date DESC"
   113→	query += fmt.Sprintf(" LIMIT $%d", argIdx)
   114→	args = append(args, limit)
   115→
   116→	rows, err := queryRows(ctx, query, args...)
   117→	if err != nil {
   118→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
   119→	}
   120→
   121→	// Get total count (with same filters)
   122→	countQuery := `SELECT count(*) AS total FROM uploads u
   123→		LEFT JOIN users usr ON u.internal_user_id = usr.id::text
   124→		WHERE 1=1`
   125→	countArgs := []any{}
   126→	countArgIdx := 1
   127→	if year != 0 {
   128→		startDate := time.Date(year, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
   129→		endDate := time.Date(year+1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
   130→		if month != 0 {
   131→			startDate = time.Date(year, time.Month(month), 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
   132→			if month == 12 {
   133→				endDate = time.Date(year+1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
   134→			} else {
   135→				endDate = time.Date(year, time.Month(month+1), 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
   136→			}
   137→		}
   138→		countQuery += fmt.Sprintf(" AND recording_date >= $%d AND recording_date < $%d", countArgIdx, countArgIdx+1)
   139→		countArgs = append(countArgs, startDate, endDate)
   140→		countArgIdx += 2
   141→	}
   142→	if detector != "" {
   143→		countQuery += fmt.Sprintf(" AND detector ILIKE $%d", countArgIdx)
   144→		countArgs = append(countArgs, "%"+detector+"%")
   145→		countArgIdx++
   146→	}
   147→	if username != "" {
   148→		countQuery += fmt.Sprintf(" AND (u.username ILIKE $%d OR usr.username ILIKE $%d OR usr.email ILIKE $%d)", countArgIdx, countArgIdx, countArgIdx)
   149→		countArgs = append(countArgs, "%"+username+"%")
   150→	}
   151→	countRow, _ := queryRow(ctx, countQuery, countArgs...)
   152→	total := 0
   153→	if countRow != nil {
   154→		if t, ok := countRow["total"]; ok {

   157→				total = int(v)

   159→				total = int(v)

   161→		}

   163→
   164→	tracks := make([]map[string]any, len(rows))

   186→		}
   187→
   188→		tracks[i] = track
   189→	}
   190→
   191→	result := map[string]any{

   214→func listTracksAPI(ctx context.Context, year, month, limit int) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
   215→	var resp map[string]any
   216→	var err error
   217→
   218→	if year != 0 && month != 0 {
   219→		resp, err = client.GetTracksByMonth(ctx, year, month)
   220→	} else if year != 0 {
   221→		resp, err = client.GetTracksByYear(ctx, year)
   222→	} else {
   223→		resp, err = client.GetTracks(ctx)
   224→	}
   225→	if err != nil {
   226→		return mcp.NewToolResultError(err.Error()), nil
   227→	}
   228→
   229→	allTracks, _ := resp["tracks"].([]any)
   230→	totalAvailable := len(allTracks)
   231→
   232→	// Sort by last_id descending so most recently uploaded tracks come first.
   233→	sort.Slice(allTracks, func(i, j int) bool {
   234→		return trackLastID(allTracks[i]) > trackLastID(allTracks[j])
   235→	})
   236→
   237→	if limit > len(allTracks) {
   238→		limit = len(allTracks)
   239→	}
   240→	limited := allTracks[:limit]
   241→
   242→	tracks := make([]map[string]any, 0, len(limited))
   243→	for _, raw := range limited {
   244→		t, ok := raw.(map[string]any)
   245→		if !ok {
   246→			continue
   247→		}
   248→		tracks = append(tracks, map[string]any{
   249→			"track_id":     t["trackID"],
   250→			"marker_count": t["markerCount"],
   251→			"first_id":     t["firstID"],
   252→			"last_id":      t["lastID"],
   253→			"index":        t["index"],
   254→			"api_url":      t["apiURL"],
   255→		})
   256→	}
   257→
   258→	result := map[string]any{
   259→		"count":           len(tracks),
   260→		"total_available": totalAvailable,
   261→		"source":          "api",
   262→		"filters": map[string]any{
   263→			"year":  nilIfZero(year),
   264→			"month": nilIfZero(month),
   265→		},

> AGENT

Successfully implemented both options to strengthen AI guidance:

## Changes Made:

### 1. Enhanced `_ai_hint` Field (Option 1)
Updated all 13 data-returning tools with more explicit prohibitions:
- **Before**: "Present all data in a purely scientific, factual manner without personal pronouns (I, we), exclamations, or conversational phrases."
- **After**: Added explicit examples and formatting guidance: "NEVER use personal pronouns (I, we, I'll, I'm, you, your), exclamations (!, Perfect, Great, Excellent), or conversational phrases. Format responses as objective statements: 'Latest reading: X CPM at location Y' NOT 'I found a reading of X CPM' or 'Perfect! The sensor shows...'."

### 2. Updated Tool Descriptions (Option 2)
Added scientific objectivity guidance to the tool definitions for 7 key measurement tools:
- [tool_device_history.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_device_history.go)
- [tool_sensor_history.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_sensor_history.go)
- [tool_sensor_current.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_sensor_current.go)
- [tool_list_sensors.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_list_sensors.go)
- [tool_query_radiation.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_query_radiation.go)
- [tool_search_area.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_search_area.go)
- [tool_get_track.go](go/cmd/mcp-server/tool_get_track.go)

Each now includes: "CRITICAL: Present all findings in an objective, scientific manner without using personal pronouns (I, we, I'll, you) or conversational language (Perfect!, Great!). Format as factual statements only."

The changes have been committed and pushed to the repository. The deployment workflow will automatically deploy these updates to simplemap.safecast.org.
